    Let $S$ be the U-statistics given in \eqref{originalUstat}. Let $Z$ be a Poisson distributed random variable with mean $\lambda$. Assume either that
    \begin{itemize}
        \item[(i)] the intensity measure $K_t \otimes \mathbb{Q}$ is finite
    \end{itemize}
    or
    \begin{itemize}
        \item[(ii)] $\mathbb{P} (\exists r_0>0 \,: \, \hat{\eta}( \mathbb{R}^d \times \cdot) = \hat{\eta}( (B(0,r) \times \cdot) \, \forall r \geq r_0 ) = 1$.
    \end{itemize}
    Then it holds
    \begin{align*}
	    \mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{Wass}}({S}, Z) \leq \left| \mathbb{E}[{S}] - \lambda \right| + \frac{2^{k+1}}{k!} \, s_t(B)
    \end{align*}
for
\begin{align*}
  &  s_t(B) := \max_{1 \leq \ell \leq k-1} 
\int \int_{X^\ell}  
\int_{X^{k - \ell}}\int_{X^{k - \ell}} \mathbf{1}\big( {f}_t ( {x}_1, \ldots, {x}_k , w ) \in B \big)  \\
&  \times \mathbf{1}\big( {f}_t ( {x}_1, \ldots, {x}_\ell, {x}'_{\ell+1} \ldots, {x}'_{k}, w ) \in B \big){K}_t^{k - \ell}(d({x}_{\ell+1}, \ldots, {x}_k)) \\
&  \times  {K}_t^{k - \ell}(d({x}'_{\ell+1}, \ldots, {x}'_k)) {K}_t^\ell(d({x}_1, \ldots, {x}_\ell)) d\mathbb{Q}^{\frac{(2k - \ell) (2k -\ell-1)}{2}} (dw) .
\end{align*}
  
\end{theorem}




\subsubsection{Compound Poisson approximation}

As another consequence of Theorem \ref{Poisson_process_approximation} we get the analogue of \cite[Theorem 7.4]{dst}. Consider the family of U-statistics

\begin{align*}
S_t := \frac{1}{k!} \sum_{(x_1, \ldots, x_k) \in \eta_{t,\neq}^k} h_t(x_1, \ldots, x_k, (W_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j =1,\ldots, k, i \neq j}), \quad t \ge 1,
\end{align*}
for a symmetric function $h_t$. Then define

\begin{align*}
L_t(A) := \frac{1}{k!} \, \mathbb{E} \sum_{(x_1, \ldots, x_k) \in \eta_{t, \neq}^k} 
\mathbf{1}\left( h_t(x_1, \ldots, x_k, (W_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j =1,\ldots, k, i \neq j}) \in t^{-\gamma} A \setminus \{0\} \right),
\end{align*}
for $A \in \mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})$. We get the following result.

\begin{theorem}\label{compound}
Let \( \zeta \) be a Poisson process on \( \mathbb{R} \) with a finite intensity measure \( M \), let
\[
Z := \sum_{x \in \zeta} x,
\]
and let \( \gamma \in \mathbb{R} \). Then, for \( t \ge 1 \),
\begin{align*}
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(t^{\gamma} S_t, Z) \le \mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(L_t, M) + \frac{2^{k+1}}{k!} \, r_t,
\end{align*}
with
\begin{align*}
  &  r_t(A) := \max_{1 \leq \ell \leq k-1} 
\int \int_{X^\ell}  
\int_{X^{k - \ell}}\int_{X^{k - \ell}} \\
& \, \mathbf{1}\big(  h_t( {x}_1, \ldots, {x}_k , w ) \neq 0  \big) \mathbf{1}\big( h_t( {x}_1, \ldots, {x}_\ell, {x}'_{\ell+1} \ldots, {x}'_{k}, w ) \neq 0 \big) \\
&\,{K}^{k - \ell}(d({x}_{\ell+1}, \ldots, {x}_k))   {K}^{k - \ell}(d({x}'_{\ell+1}, \ldots, {x}'_k)) {K}^\ell(d({x}_1, \ldots, {x}_\ell)) d\mathbb{Q}^{\frac{(2k - \ell) (2k -\ell-1)}{2}} (dw) .
\end{align*}
\end{theorem}








\subsubsection{Approximation by $\alpha$-stable distribution}

Fix $\alpha \in (0, 1)$ and $\gamma \in \mathbb{R}$. The goal is to make use of a functional limit theorem for the point processes
\begin{align*}
    t^\gamma \cdot \xi_t &:= \frac{1}{k!} \sum_{(x_1, \ldots, x_k) \in \eta^k_{\neq}} 
\mathbf{1}(h_t(x_1, \ldots, x_k,(W_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j =1,\ldots, k, i \neq j}) \ne 0) \\
&\quad \delta_{\operatorname{sign}(h_t(x_1, \ldots, x_k,(W_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j =1,\ldots, k, i \neq j})) t^\gamma |h_t(x_1, \ldots, x_k,(W_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j =1,\ldots, k, i \neq j})|^{-\alpha}},
\end{align*}
 $t \ge 1$, on $\mathbb{R}$, with $\operatorname{sign}(a) = \mathbf{1}(a \ge 0) - \mathbf{1}(a < 0)$. In fact, having established Theorem~\ref{Poisson_process_approximation} the following application follows from the proof of \cite[Theorem 7.8]{dst}. In the following we denote by $L_t$ the intensity measure of $t^\gamma \cdot \xi_t$, and

\begin{align*}
  &  r_t(A) := \max_{1 \leq \ell \leq k-1} 
\int \int_{X^\ell}  
\int_{X^{k - \ell}}\int_{X^{k - \ell}} \\
& \quad \mathbf{1}\big(  h_t( {x}_1, \ldots, {x}_k , w ) \neq 0  \big) \mathbf{1}\left( \operatorname{sign}(h_t(x_1, \ldots, x_k,w)) \, |h_t(x_1, \ldots, x_k,w)|^{-\alpha} \in t^{-\gamma} A \right)
 \\
&  \times \mathbf{1}\big( h_t( {x}_1, \ldots, {x}_\ell, {x}'_{\ell+1} \ldots, {x}'_{k}, w ) \neq 0 \big) \\
& \times \mathbf{1}\left( \operatorname{sign}(h_t({x}_1, \ldots, {x}_\ell, {x}'_{\ell+1} \ldots, {x}'_{k},w)) \, |h_t({x}_1, \ldots, {x}_\ell, {x}'_{\ell+1} \ldots, {x}'_{k},w)|^{-\alpha} \in t^{-\gamma} A \right)\\
&\,{K}^{k - \ell}(d({x}_{\ell+1}, \ldots, {x}_k))   {K}^{k - \ell}(d({x}'_{\ell+1}, \ldots, {x}'_k)) {K}^\ell(d({x}_1, \ldots, {x}_\ell)) d\mathbb{Q}^{\frac{(2k - \ell) (2k -\ell-1)}{2}} (dw) .
\end{align*}

\begin{theorem}\label{stable}
Let \( \alpha \in (0, 1) \), and let \( M \) be either the Lebesgue measure on \( \mathbb{R} \) or its restriction to \( \mathbb{R}_+ \). Define
\[
Z := \sum_{x \in \zeta} \operatorname{sign}(x) |x|^{-1/\alpha},
\]
where \( \zeta \) is a Poisson process with intensity measure \( M\). Assume that there exist a constant \( \gamma \in \mathbb{R} \) and functions \( g_1, g_2, g_3 \colon \mathbb{R}_+^2 \to \mathbb{R}_+ \) such that, for every \( a > 0 \) and \( t \ge 1 \),
\begin{align*}
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{TV}}(L_t|_{[-a,a]}, M|_{[-a,a]}) &\le g_1(a, t), \\
r_t([-a,a]) &\le g_2(a, t), \\
\frac{t^{-\gamma/\alpha}}{k!} \, \mathbb{E} \sum_{(x_1, \ldots, x_k) \in \eta^k_{\neq}}
\frac{\mathbf{1}(|h_t(x_1, \ldots, x_k,(W_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j =1,\ldots, k, i \neq j})| < t^{\gamma/\alpha} a^{-1/\alpha})}{|h_t(x_1, \ldots, x_k,(W_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j =1,\ldots, k, i \neq j})|}
&\le g_3(a, t).
\end{align*}
Then there exists a constant \( C > 0 \), depending only on \( \alpha \) and \( k \), such that
\[
	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{K}}(t^{-\gamma/\alpha} S_t, Z) \le C g(t), \quad t \ge 1,
\]
where
\[
g(t) := \inf_{a > 0} \max\left\{ a^{1/2 - 1/(2\alpha)},\; g_1(a,t),\; g_2(a,t),\; \sqrt{g_3(a,t)} \right\}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\fi

\subsection{Moderate deviations and concentration inequalities}
Let us first recall from \cite{DemboZeitouni2010} that a sequence of probability measures $\{\mathbb{P}_n\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ on a topological space $\mathcal{Z}$ with $\sigma$-algebra $\mathscr{L}$ satisfies a large deviation principle (LDP) with speed $t_n\to\infty$ and good rate function $\mathcal{I}$ if the level sets $\{z\in\mathcal{Z}:\mathcal{I}(z)\le a\}$ are compact for all $0\le a<\infty$ and if for all $A\in\mathscr{L}$,
\begin{equation*}
	-\inf_{z\in\operatorname{int}(A)}\mathcal{I}(z)\le\liminf_{n\to\infty}t_n^{-1}\log\mathbb{P}_n(A)\le\limsup_{n\to\infty}t_n^{-1}\log\mathbb{P}_n(A)\le-\inf_{z\in\operatorname{cl}(A)}\mathcal{I}(z),
\end{equation*}
where $\operatorname{int}(A)$ and $\operatorname{cl}(A)$ stand for the interior and the closure of $A$, respectively. 
A sequence of random variables $\{X_n\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ satisfies a LDP if their distributions do. We will speak about a moderate deviation principle (MDP) instead of a LDP if the scaling of the involved random variables is between that of a law of large numbers and that of a central limit theorem.

Let $\{X_n\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ be a sequence of square-integrable random variables, $\beta\ge0$ be a constant and $\{\Delta_n\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ be a positive real-valued sequence. For the ease of notation, we say that $\{X_n\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ satisfies
\begin{itemize}
	\item \textbf{$\mathrm{MDP}$}$(\beta,\{\Delta_n\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}})$
		if for any positive real-valued sequence $\{a_n\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ with
\begin{equation*}
  \lim_{n \to \infty} a_n = \infty
\qquad \text{and} \qquad
\lim_{n \to \infty} \frac{a_n}{\Delta_n^{1/(1+2\beta)}} = 0,
\end{equation*}
the rescaled random variables
$\left\{ a_n^{-1} \frac{X_n - \mathbb{E} X_n}{\sqrt{\operatorname{Var} X_n}} \right\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$
satisfy a MDP with speed $a_n^2$ and good rate function $\mathcal{I}(z) = \frac{z^2}{2}.$
\item \textbf{$\mathrm{CI}$}$(\beta, \{\Delta_n\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}})$ 
	if the Bernstein-type
concentration inequality
\begin{equation*}
\mathbb{P}\!\left( |X_n - \mathbb{E} X_n| \ge z \sqrt{\Var(X_n)} \right)
\le 2\exp\!\left(-\frac{1}{4}\min \left\{\frac{z^2}{2^{1+\beta}},\,(z \Delta_n)^{1/(1+\beta)}\right\}\right)
\end{equation*}
holds for all $n \in \mathbb{N}$ and $z \ge 0$.
    \item \textbf{$\mathrm{NACC}$}$(\beta, \{\Delta_n\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}})$ if a normal approximation bound with Cramér correction holds, that is, if there exist constants $c_0, c_1, c_2 > 0$ only depending on $\beta$ such that for all $n \in \mathbb{N}$ and $z \in \left[0, c_0 \Delta_n^{1/(1+2\beta)}\right]$,
    $$
    \mathbb{P}(X_n - \mathbb{E}X_n \geq z \sqrt{\Var X_n}) = e^{L_{n,z}^+} (1 - \Phi(z)) \left( 1 + c_1 \theta_{n,z}^+ \frac{1+z}{\Delta_n^{1/(1+2\beta)}} \right)
    $$ 
    and
    $$
    \mathbb{P}(X_n - \mathbb{E}X_n \leq -z \sqrt{\Var X_n}) = e^{L_{n,z}^-} (1 - \Phi(z)) \left( 1 + c_1 \theta_{n,z}^- \frac{1+z}{\Delta_n^{1/(1+2\beta)}} \right)
    $$ 
    with $\theta_{n,z}^+, \theta_{n,z}^- \in [-1, 1]$ and $L_{n,z}^+, L_{n,z}^- \in (-c_2 z^3 / \Delta_n^{1/(1+2\beta)}, c_2 z^3 / \Delta_n^{1/(1+2\beta)})$, where $\Phi$ is the distribution function of a standard Gaussian random variable.
\end{itemize}

The next result extends \cite[Theorem~3.2]{schulte2} from sums of Poisson $U$-statistics to $U$-statistics of edge-marked Poisson processes. 
%adding balanced condition for non-integrable subgraph count and refine the normal approximation

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:MDP}
	Let $\{\eta_n\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ be a family of Poisson processes over a  $\sigma$-finite measure space $(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{X},\lambda_n)$, and let $f_n: \mathbb{X}^k\times \mathbb{M}^{k\times k}_{\neq}\to\mathbb{R}$ be a measurable function, $n\in\mathbb{N}$.
Define 
$$S_n:=\sum_{(x_1,\hdots,x_k)\in(\eta_n)^k_{\neq}} f_n(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (\mathcal{M}_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j\in[k], i\neq j}),$$
for $n\in\mathbb{N}$.
Suppose that $\E\left(\overline{S}_n^\ell\right)<\infty$ for all $\ell\ge1$ with $\overline{S}_n$ given in \eqref{def-integ1}. 
Let $X_n:=$ for $n\in\mathbb{N}$. Suppose that there is a positive real-valuled sequence $\{b_n\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ such that
\begin{equation}
	(\Var S_n)^{-\ell/2}\left|\int_{\mathbb{X}^{|\pi|}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}(f_n^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathbb{Q}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}(\mathrm{d}\bm{u})\lambda_n^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x})\right|\le b_n^{\ell-2}
\end{equation}
for all $\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^\ell(k)$, $\ell\ge3$ and $n\in\mathbb{N}$. Then $\{S_n\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ satisfies 
\textbf{$\mathrm{MDP}$}$(k-1,\{(b_nk^{3k})^{-1}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}})$, 
\textbf{$\mathrm{CI}$}$(k-1, \{(b_nk^{3k})^{-1}\}_{n \in \mathbb{N}})$ and \textbf{$\mathrm{NACC}$}$(k-1,\{(b_nk^{3k})^{-1}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}})$. 
\end{theorem}


\section{Proofs of main results}
\subsection{Approximation results}\label{sec:approximation}
%\subsection{Construction of edge-marked Poisson processes}

\iffalse
Let $(\mathbb{X},\mathcal{X})$ be a measurable space and let $\eta$ be a Poisson process on $\mathbb{X}$ with a $\sigma$-finite non-atomic intensity measure $\lambda$. We assume that $\eta$ can be almost surely written as 
$$
\eta=\sum_{i\in I} \delta_{X_i}
$$
with random elements $(X_i)_{i\in I}$ on $\mathbb{X}$ and a random index set $I\subseteq\mathbb{N}$, where $\delta_x$ denotes the Dirac measure concentrated on $x$. 

Moreover, let $(\mathbb{M},\mathcal{M})$ be a measurable space with a probability measure $\mathbb{Q}$. Let $(M_{i,j})_{i,j\in\mathbb{N},i\neq j}$ be a family of independent random elements on $\mathbb{M}$ distributed according to $\mathbb{Q}$. Now we assign the mark $M_{i,j}$ to the pair $(X_i,X_j)$ for $i,j\in I$ with $i\neq j$. For $y,z\in\eta$ with $y\neq z$ we denote by $M_{y,z}$ the mark of the pair $(y,z)$. Note that the pairs $(y,z)$ and $(z,y)$ have different marks.

For $k\in\mathbb{N}$ we use the short hand notation $[k]:=\{1,\hdots,k\}$ and define
$$
\mathbb{M}^{k\times k}_{\neq}:=\{(u_{i,j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j}: u_{i,j}\in \mathbb{M} \text{ for } i,j\in[k],i\neq j\}.
$$
Moreover let $\eta^k_{\neq}$ be the set of all $k$-tuples of distinct points of $\eta$.

For $f: \mathbb{X}^k\times \mathbb{M}^{k\times k}_{\neq}\to\mathbb{R}$ with $k\in\mathbb{N}$ we are interested in random variables of the following form:
\begin{equation}\label{def:ustat}
S=\sum_{(x_1,\hdots,x_k)\in\eta^k_{\neq}} f(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (M_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j\in[k], i\neq j}).
\end{equation}
We also denote 
\begin{equation}\label{def-integ1}
    \overline{S}:=\sum_{(x_1,\hdots,x_k)\in\eta^k_{\neq}} \left|f(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (M_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j\in[k], i\neq j})\right|.
\end{equation}
\fi
Next we construct a random variable with the same distribution as $S$ that is a function of an underlying Poisson process on a larger space. 
\iffalse
For this we assume that for each $n\in\mathbb{N}$ there exists a partition $(A_\ell^{(n)})_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}$ of $\mathbb{X}$ such that the following conditions are satisfied:
\begin{itemize}
\item For $x\in\mathbb{X}$ and $n\in\mathbb{N}$ let $A^{(n)}(x)$ be the unique $A^{(n)}_\ell$ such that $x\in A^{(n)}_\ell$. Then,
\begin{equation}\label{eqn:monotonicity_partition}
A^{(n)}(x)\subseteq A^{(m)}(x)
\end{equation}
for all $x\in\mathbb{X}$ and $m,n\in\mathbb{N}$ with $m\leq n$.
\item There exists a sequence $(\varepsilon_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{eqn:size_partition}
\lambda(A_\ell^{(n)}) \leq \frac{\varepsilon_n}{\ell}
\end{equation}
for all $\ell,n\in\mathbb{N}$ and $\lim_{n\to\infty}\varepsilon_n=0$.
\item There exists a measurable function $\operatorname{dist}:\mathbb{X}\times \mathbb{X}\to[0,\infty)$ such that 
\begin{equation}\label{eqn:uniqueness_distances}
\lambda^3\left(\left\{(x,y,z)\in \mathbb{X}\times A^{(n)}_\ell\times A_\ell^{(n)}: \operatorname{dist}(x,y)=\operatorname{dist}(x,z)\right\}\right)=0.
\end{equation}
for all $\ell,n\in\mathbb{N}$.
\end{itemize}
\fi

We define $\widehat{\mathbb{X}}:=\mathbb{X}\times \mathbb{M}^{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}$ and $\widehat{\lambda}:=\lambda\otimes \mathbb{Q}^{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}$. Let $\widehat{\eta}$ be a Poisson process on $\widehat{\mathbb{X}}$ with intensity measure $\widehat{\lambda}$.

Let $n\in\mathbb{N}$ and let $(x,(u_{i,j})_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}})$ and $(y,(v_{i,j})_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}})$ be distinct points of $\widehat{\eta}$. Suppose that $y\in A^{(n)}_\ell$ for some $\ell\in\mathbb{N}$. Now we order the points of $\widehat{\eta}$ whose projections to $\mathbb{X}$ are in $A^{(n)}_\ell$ according to the distances of their projections to $\mathbb{X}$ to $x$ with respect to the distance function $\operatorname{dist}$. Note that the assumption \eqref{eqn:uniqueness_distances} ensures that this ordering is almost surely unique. If $y$ is the $k$-th closest point to $x$ in $A^{(n)}_\ell$, then we define 
\begin{equation}
	\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(n)}_{x,y}:=u_{\ell,k}.
\end{equation}
Let $\eta_{\mathbb{X}}$ be the projection of $\widehat{\eta}$ to $\mathbb{X}$ and denote the projection of $\hat{x}\in\widehat{\mathbb{X}}$ to $\mathbb{X}$ by $x$. Then, we have
$$
(\eta_{\mathbb{X}}, (\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(n)}_{x,y})_{x,y\in\eta_{\mathbb{X}}, x\ne y}) \overset{d}{=} (\eta, (\mathcal{M}_{x,y})_{x,y\in\eta,x\neq y}),
$$
where $\eta$ and $(\mathcal{M}_{x,y})_{x,y\in\eta,x\neq y}$ are the Poisson process and the marks introduced in Subsection \ref{subsec:edge-marked_PP}.
This implies that
\begin{equation}
	\widetilde{S}^{(n)} := \sum_{(x_1,\hdots,x_k)\in(\eta_{\mathbb{X}})^k_{\neq}} f(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(n)}_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j}) %= \sum_{(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k)\in\widehat{\eta}^k_{\neq}} f(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (\widetilde{M}^{(n)}_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j})
\end{equation}
and
$$
\widetilde{\xi}^{(n)} := \frac{1}{k!} \sum_{(x_1,\hdots,x_k)\in(\eta_{\mathbb{X}})^k_{\neq}} \mathbf{1}((x_1,\hdots,x_k, (\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(n)}_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j})\in\operatorname{dom} g) \delta_{g(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (\widetilde{M}^{(n)}_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j})}
$$
have the same distributions as $S$ and $\xi$.

For $n\in\mathbb{N}$ and any distinct points $\hat{x}=(x,(u_{i,j})_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}})$ and $\hat{y}=(y,(v_{i,j})_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}})$ of $\widehat{\eta}$, we define 
\begin{equation}
	\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{(n)}_{x,y}:=u_{\ell,1},~~~~~\mbox{if}~~~ y\in A^{(n)}_\ell. 
\end{equation}
Note that $\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{(n)}_{x,y}$, in contrast to $\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(n)}_{x,y}$, only depends $\hat{x}$ and $\hat{y}$. Thus, for each $n\in\mathbb{N}$ there exists a function $\tilde{m}^{(n)}:\widehat{\mathbb{X}}^k\to\mathbb{M}^{k\times k}_{\neq}$ such that
for all $(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k)\in\widehat{\eta}^k_{\neq}$,
$$
\tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k)=(\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{(n)}_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j}.
$$
To be more clear, let $\hat{x}_i=(x_i,(u^{i}_{\imath,j})_{\imath,j\in\mathbb{N}})$, $i\in[k]$, with $x_i\in A^{(n)}_{\ell_i}$ for some $\ell_i\in\mathbb{N}$, $i\in[k]$. It gives that  
$$
\tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k)=(\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{(n)}_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j}=(u^{i}_{\ell_j,1})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j}.
$$
Next we define
\begin{equation}\label{Ustat1}
\widehat{S}^{(n)} := \sum_{(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k)\in\widehat{\eta}^k_{\neq}} f(x_1,\hdots,x_k, \tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k))
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{xi_hat_n}
\widehat{\xi}^{(n)} := \frac{1}{k!} \sum_{(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k)\in\widehat{\eta}^k_{\neq}} \mathbf{1}\{ (x_1,\hdots,x_k, \tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k))\in\operatorname{dom} f \} \delta_{g(x_1,\hdots,x_k, \tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k))}.
\end{equation}
Note that $\widehat{S}^{(n)}$ is a Poisson $U$-statistic of order $k$ as each summand only depends on the $k$ distinct points $\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k$. Similarly a point of $\widehat{\xi}^{(n)}$ depends only on $k$ distinct points $\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k$. 

For $n\in\mathbb{N}$ observe that $\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{(n)}_{x,y}=\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{(n)}_{x,y}$ if $\eta_{\mathbb{X}}(A^{(n)}_\ell)\leq 1$ for all $\ell\in\mathbb{N}$. As identical edge marks imply $\widetilde{S}^{(n)}=\widehat{S}^{(n)}$, we see that
\begin{align}
\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{S}^{(n)}\neq\widehat{S}^{(n)}) & \leq \mathbb{P}(\eta_{\mathbb{X}}(A^{(n)}_\ell)\geq 2 \text{ for some } \ell\in\mathbb{N})\nonumber \\
& \leq \sum_{\ell=1}^\infty \mathbb{P}(\eta_{\mathbb{X}}(A^{(n)}_\ell)\geq 2) \leq \sum_{\ell=1}^\infty \lambda(A^{(n)}_\ell)^2 \leq \varepsilon_n^2 \sum_{\ell=1}^\infty \frac{1}{\ell^2}.\label{diff1}
\end{align}
The same argument leads to
\begin{equation}\label{diff2}
\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(n)}\neq\widehat{\xi}^{(n)}) \leq \varepsilon_n^2 \sum_{\ell=1}^\infty \frac{1}{\ell^2}.
\end{equation}

In the following we denote by $\mathbb{Q}^{m\times m}_{\neq}$ with $m\in\mathbb{N}$ the distribution of $(Y_{i,j})_{i,j\in[k], i\neq j}$ for independent $Y_{i,j}$, $i,j\in[m]$, $i\neq j$, distributed according to $\mathbb{Q}$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lma1}
Let $h: \mathbb{X}^m\times \mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{m\times m}\to \mathbb{R}$ with $m\in\mathbb{N}$ be such that $\|h\|_\infty<\infty$ and
$$
\lambda^m(\{ (x_1,\hdots,x_m)\in\mathbb{X}^m: h(x_1,\hdots,x_k,u)\neq 0 \text{ for some } u\in\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{m\times m} \})<\infty.
$$
Then,
\begin{align*}
& \lim_{n\to\infty} \int_{\widehat{\mathbb{X}}^m} h(x_1,\hdots,x_m, \tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_m)) \, \widehat{\lambda}^m(\mathrm{d}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_m) ) \\
& = \int_{\mathbb{X}^m} \int_{\mathbb{M}^{m\times m}_{\neq}} h(x_1,\hdots,x_m, u) \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{m\times m}(\mathrm{d}u) \, \lambda^m(\mathrm{d}(x_1,\hdots,x_m)).
\end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Define
$$
\Delta^{(n)}_m := \{ (x_1,\hdots,x_m) \in \mathbb{X}^m: \exists~\ell\in\mathbb{N}, i,j\in[m], i\ne j\text{ with $x_i,x_j\in A^{(n)}_{\ell}$}\}
$$
and
$$
\widehat{\Delta}^{(n)}_m := \{ (\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_m) \in \widehat{\mathbb{X}}^m:  \exists~\ell\in\mathbb{N}, i,j\in[m], i\ne j\text{ with $x_i,x_j\in A^{(n)}_{\ell}$}\}.
$$
For $(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_m)\in\widehat{\mathbb{X}}^m\backslash\widehat{\Delta}_m^{(n)}$, $\tilde{m}^{(n)}$ assigns different marks to each pair $(i,j)$ with $i,j\in[m]$ and $i\neq j$ so that
\begin{align*}
& \int_{\widehat{\mathbb{X}}^m\backslash\widehat{\Delta}_m^{(n)}} h(x_1,\hdots,x_m, \tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_m)) \, \widehat{\lambda}^m(\mathrm{d}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_m) ) \\
& = \int_{\mathbb{X}^m\backslash\Delta_m^{(n)}} \int_{\mathbb{M}^{m\times m}_{\neq}} h(x_1,\hdots,x_m, u) \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{m\times m}(\mathrm{d}u) \, \lambda^m(\mathrm{d}(x_1,\hdots,x_m)).
\end{align*}
It follows from \eqref{eqn:monotonicity_partition} and \eqref{eqn:size_partition} that
$$
\mathbf{1}\{ (\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_m)\in\widehat{\mathbb{X}}^m\backslash\widehat{\Delta}_m^{(n)}\} \to 1 \quad \text{as} \quad n\to \infty \quad \text{for} \quad \widehat{\lambda}^m\text{-a.e. } (\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_m)\in\widehat{\mathbb{X}}^m
$$
and
$$
\mathbf{1}\{ (x_1,\hdots,x_m)\in\mathbb{X}^m\backslash\Delta_m^{(n)}\} \to 1 \quad \text{as} \quad n\to \infty \quad \text{for} \quad \lambda^m\text{-a.e. } (x_1,\hdots,x_m)\in\mathbb{X}^m.
$$
Due to the boundedness of $h$ and the assumptions on its support we can apply the dominated convergence theorem and obtain
\begin{align*}
& \lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\widehat{\mathbb{X}}^m\backslash\widehat{\Delta}_m^{(n)}} h(x_1,\hdots,x_m, \tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_m)) \, \widehat{\lambda}^m(\mathrm{d}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_m) ) \\
& = \lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{X}^m\backslash\Delta_m^{(n)}} \int_{\mathbb{M}^{m\times m}_{\neq}} h(x_1,\hdots,x_m, u) \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{m\times m}(\mathrm{d}u) \, \lambda^m(\mathrm{d}(x_1,\hdots,x_m)) \\ & = \int_{\mathbb{X}^m} \int_{\mathbb{M}^{m\times m}_{\neq}} h(x_1,\hdots,x_m, u) \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{m\times m}(\mathrm{d}u) \, \lambda^m(\mathrm{d}(x_1,\hdots,x_m))
\end{align*}
and
$$
\lim_{n\to\infty} \int_{ \widehat{\Delta}^{(n)}_m} h(x_1,\hdots,x_m, \tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_m)) \, \widehat{\lambda}^m(\mathrm{d}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_m) )=0.
$$
Combining the two limits proves the statement.
\end{proof}
For the proof of Theorem~\ref{normalapp1}, we prepare ourselves with the following results.
We first recall the following estimates from \cite[Theorem~4.7]{reitznerschulte} and \cite[Theorem~4.2]{schulte} after adapting a few of notation. 

Let $\widetilde{\Pi}_{\ge2}(i,i,j,j)$ be the collection of all partitions $\pi$ of 
$$x_1^{(1)},\dots,x_i^{(1)},x_1^{(2)},\dots,x_i^{(2)},x_1^{(3)},\dots,x_j^{(3)},x_1^{(4)},\dots,x_j^{(4)},$$
such that 
\begin{itemize}
\item two variables with the same upper index are in different blocks of $\pi$;
\item each block of $\pi$ includes at least two variables;
\item there is no disjoint sets $A_1,A_2$ with $A_1\cup A_2=[4]$ such that each block of $\pi$ either consists of variables with upper index in $A_1$ or of variables with upper index in $A_2$.
\end{itemize}
Let $g_1,g_2:X^i\to\R$ and $g_3,g_4:X^j\to\R$, the tensor product $g_1\otimes g_2\otimes g_3\otimes g_4:X^{2i+2j}\to\R$ is defined by 
\begin{eqnarray*}
&&(g_1\otimes g_2\otimes g_3\otimes g_4)(x_1^{(1)},\dots,x_j^{(4)})\\
&=&g_1(x_1^{(1)},\dots,x_{i}^{(1)})g_2(x_1^{(2)},\dots,x_{i}^{(2)})g_3(x_1^{(3)},\dots,x_{j}^{(3)})g_4(x_1^{(4)},\dots,x_{j}^{(4)}).
\end{eqnarray*}
For $\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}_{\ge2}(i,i,j,j)$, denote $|\pi|$ the number of blocks in $\pi$, and the function $(g_1\otimes g_2\otimes g_3\otimes g_4)_\pi:X^{|\pi|}\to\R$ is defined by replacing all variables in the same block of $\pi$ by a new common variable.
\begin{prop}\label{normalU-1}
Let $F\in L^2(\mathbb{P}_\eta)$ be an absolutely convergent $U$-statistics of order $k$ with variance $\Var F>0$ and let $N$ be a standard Gaussian random variable. Then 
\begin{eqnarray}
	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{Wass}}\left(\frac{F-\E F}{\sqrt{\Var F}},N\right)&\le&2k^{7/2}\sum_{1\le i\le j\le k}\frac{\sqrt{M_{ij}(f)}}{\Var F},\\
	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{K}}\left(\frac{F-\E F}{\sqrt{\Var F}},N\right)&\le&19k^5\sum_{i,j=1}^k\frac{\sqrt{M_{ij}(f)}}{\Var F}
\end{eqnarray}
where 
\begin{equation}
    M_{ij}(f):=\sum_{\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}_{\ge2}(i,i,j,j)}\int_{X^{|\pi|}}(\overline{f}_i\otimes\overline{f}_i\otimes\overline{f}_j\otimes\overline{f}_j)_{\pi}\mathrm{d}\mu^{|\pi|},
\end{equation}
and 
\begin{equation}
    \overline{f}_i(x_1,\dots,x_i):={k\choose i}\int_{X^{k-i}}|f(x_1,\dots,x_i,y_1,\dots,y_{k-i})|\mu^{k-i}(\mathrm{d}(y_1,\dots,y_{k-i})),
\end{equation}
for $i=1,\dots,k$ and $f_i=0$ for $i>k$.
\end{prop}
%Using \cite[Lemma~6.2]{Last_Nestmann_Schulte}
We prepare the proof of Theorem~\ref{normalapp1} with the following lemmas.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:limits_S_n}
    Let $f: \mathbb{X}^k\times \mathbb{M}^{k\times k}_{\neq}\to\mathbb{R}$ be a measurable function satisfying that $\supp(f)\subseteq B^k\times\mathbb{M}^{k\times k}_{\ne}$ with $\lambda(B)<\infty$ and $\|f\|_{\infty}<\infty$.  
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item[1)] For $p>0$, we have $\E\left[\left|S-\widehat{S}^{(n)}\right|^p\right]\to0$, as $n\to\infty$.
        \item[2)] For $\ell\in\N$, we have 
       \begin{equation} \lim_{n\to\infty}\E\left[(\widehat{S}^{(n)})^{\ell}\right]=\E\left[S^{\ell}\right],
       \end{equation}
       and 
       \begin{eqnarray}
        \E[S^{\ell}]=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^\ell(k)}\int_{\mathbb{X}^{|\pi|}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}(f^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathbb{Q}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}(\mathrm{d}\bm{u})\lambda^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x}).
       \end{eqnarray}
    \end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\begin{enumerate}
        \item[1)] 
According to the coupling $(\widetilde{S}^{(n)},\widehat{S}^{(n)})$ constructed in previous section, together with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and \eqref{diff2} it follows that 
\begin{eqnarray*}
    &&\E\left[\left|S-\widehat{S}^{(n)}\right|^p\right]\\
    &=&\E\left[\left|\widetilde{S}^{(n)}-\widehat{S}^{(n)}\right|^p\mathbf{1}_{\{\widetilde{S}^{(n)}\ne\widehat{S}^{(n)}\}}\right]\\
    &=&\E\left[\left|\sum_{(x_1,\hdots,x_k)\in(\eta_{\mathbb{X}})^k_{\neq}} f(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (\widetilde{M}^{(n)}_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j})-\widehat{S}^{(n)}\right|^p\mathbf{1}_{\{\widetilde{S}^{(n)}\ne\widehat{S}^{(n)}\}}\right]\\
    &=&\E\left[\left|\sum_{(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k)\in\widehat{\eta}^k_{\neq}} f(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (\widetilde{M}^{(n)}_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j})-\widehat{S}^{(n)}\right|^p\mathbf{1}_{\{\widetilde{S}^{(n)}\ne\widehat{S}^{(n)}\}}\right]\\
    &=&\E\left[\left|\sum_{(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k)\in\widehat{\eta}^k_{\neq}} \left[f(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (\widetilde{M}^{(n)}_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j})-f(x_1,\hdots,x_k, \tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k))\right]\right|^p\mathbf{1}_{\{\widetilde{S}^{(n)}\ne\widehat{S}^{(n)}\}}\right]\\
    &\le&(2^p\|f\|_{\infty}^p\vee 1)\E\left[\eta(B)^{kp}\mathbf{1}_{\{\widetilde{S}^{(n)}\ne\widehat{S}^{(n)}\}}\right]\\
    &\le&(2^p\|f\|_{\infty}^p\vee 1)\left(\E\eta(B)^{2kp}\right)^{1/2}\left(\IP(\widetilde{S}^{(n)}\ne\widehat{S}^{(n)})\right)^{1/2}\\
    &\le&(2^p\|f\|_{\infty}^p\vee 1)\left(\E\eta(B)^{2kp}\right)^{1/2}\varepsilon_n \sqrt{\sum_{\ell=1}^\infty \frac{1}{\ell^2} }.
\end{eqnarray*}
Therefore, for $p>0$, we have $\lim_{n\to0}\E\left[\left|S-\widehat{S}^{(n)}\right|^p\right]=0$.
\item[2)]
For $\ell\in\N$, $\lim_{n\to0}\E\left[\left|S-\widehat{S}^{(n)}\right|^{\ell}\right]=0$ implies $$\E\left[S^{\ell}\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\E\left[(\widehat{S}^{(n)})^{\ell}\right].$$ On the other hand, we have 
\begin{eqnarray*}
\E\left[(\widehat{S}^{(n)})^{\ell}\right]&=&\E\left[\left(\sum_{(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k)\in\widehat{\eta}^k_{\neq}} f(x_1,\hdots,x_k, \tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_k))\right)^{\ell}\right]\\
&=&\E\left[\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^\ell(k)}\sum_{(\hat{x}_1,\dots,\hat{x}_{|\pi|})\in\hat{\eta}^{|\pi|}_{\ne}}(f^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(x_1,\dots,x_{|\pi|},\tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_{|\pi|}))\right]\\
&=&\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^\ell(k)}\left[\int_{\widehat{\mathbb{X}}^{|\pi|}\backslash\widehat{\Delta}_{|\pi|}^{(n)}} (f^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(x_1,\dots,x_{|\pi|},\tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_{|\pi|})) \, \widehat{\lambda}^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_{|\pi|}) )\right.\\
&&\left.\ \ \ \ \ +\int_{\widehat{\Delta}_{|\pi|}^{(n)}}(f^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(x_1,\dots,x_{|\pi|},\tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_{|\pi|})) \, \widehat{\lambda}^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_{|\pi|}) )
\right]\\
&=&\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^\ell(k)}\left[\int_{\mathbb{X}^{|\pi|}\backslash\Delta_{|\pi|}^{(n)}} \int_{\mathbb{M}^{|\pi|\times |\pi|}_{\neq}} (f^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(x_1,\dots,x_{|\pi|},u) \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{|\pi|\times |\pi|}(\mathrm{d}u) \, \lambda^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}(x_1,\hdots,x_{|\pi|}))\right.\\
&&\left.\ \ \ \ \ +\int_{\widehat{\Delta}_{|\pi|}^{(n)}}(f^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(x_1,\dots,x_{|\pi|},\tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_{|\pi|})) \, \widehat{\lambda}^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}(\hat{x}_1,\hdots,\hat{x}_{|\pi|}) )
\right].
\end{eqnarray*}
Then, by Lemma~\ref{lma1}, we can see that 
\begin{eqnarray*}
    \E\left[S^{\ell}\right]=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^\ell(k)}\int_{\mathbb{X}^{|\pi|}} \int_{\mathbb{M}^{|\pi|\times |\pi|}_{\neq}} (f^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(x_1,\dots,x_{|\pi|},u) \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{|\pi|\times |\pi|}(\mathrm{d}u) \, \lambda^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}(x_1,\hdots,x_{|\pi|})).
\end{eqnarray*}
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}\label{lem:limits_S_n2}
   Let $f: \mathbb{X}^k\times \mathbb{M}^{k\times k}_{\neq}\to\mathbb{R}$ be a measurable function. 
\begin{enumerate}
\item[1)] Suppose $f\ge0$ and $\E[S^{\ell}]<\infty$ for some $\ell\in\N$, then there exists a sequence of functions $(f_m)_{m\in\N}$ such that 
\begin{equation}
    \lim_{m\to\infty}\E\left[\left|S-S_m\right|^{\ell}\right]=0,
\end{equation}
with 
$$S_m:=\sum_{(x_1,\hdots,x_k)\in\eta^k_{\neq}} f_m(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (\mathcal{M}_{x_i,x_j})_{i,j\in[k], i\neq j}),$$
and 
\begin{equation}
\E[S^{\ell}]=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^\ell(k)}\int_{\mathbb{X}^{|\pi|}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}(f^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathbb{Q}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}(\mathrm{d}\bm{u})\lambda^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x}).
\end{equation}
\item[2)]Suppose that $\E\left(\overline{S}^{\ell}\right)<\infty$ for some $\ell\in\N$ with $\overline{S}$ given in \eqref{def-integ1}. Then, the above results prevail. 
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Because $\lambda$ is $\sigma$-finite, 
there exists an increasing sequence of measurable sets $(B_m)_{m\in\mathbb{N}}$ such that $\lambda(B_m)<\infty$ for each $m\in\mathbb{N}$ and $ \cup_{m=1}^\infty B_m=\mathbb{X}$. Take $f_m:\mathbb{X}^k\times \mathbb{M}^{k\times k}_{\neq}\to\mathbb{R}$ defined by 
\begin{equation*}
f_m(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (u_{i,j})_{i,j\in[k], i\neq j}):=f(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (u_{i,j})_{i,j\in[k], i\neq j})\mathbf{1}_{\{(x_1,\hdots,x_k)\in B_m\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|f|\le m\}}.
\end{equation*}
It is clear that $f_m$ converges to $f$ as $m\to\infty$ pointwisely.
Let $S_m$ be defined by replacing $f$ with $f_m$ in \eqref{def:ustat}. 
\begin{enumerate}
\item[1)] 
From Lemma~\ref{lem:limits_S_n}, we have 
\begin{equation}
    \E[(S_m)^\ell]=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^\ell(k)}\int_{\mathbb{X}^{|\pi|}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}(f_m^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathbb{Q}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}(\mathrm{d}\bm{u})\lambda^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x}).
\end{equation}
When $f\ge0$, it gives $0\le f-f_m\le f$. Therefore, by the dominated convergence theorem, we have $\lim_{m\to\infty}\E\left[\left|S-S_m\right|^{\ell}\right]=0$. It further provides that
\begin{eqnarray*}
    \E[S^\ell]&=&\lim_{m\to\infty}\E[(S_m)^\ell]\\
    &=&\lim_{m\to\infty}\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^\ell(k)}\int_{\mathbb{X}^{|\pi|}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}(f_m^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathbb{Q}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}(\mathrm{d}\bm{u})\lambda^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x})\\
    &=&\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^\ell(k)}\int_{\mathbb{X}^{|\pi|}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}(f^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathbb{Q}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}(\mathrm{d}\bm{u})\lambda^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x}).
\end{eqnarray*}
\item[2)] For general $f$ such that $\E\left(\overline{S}^{\ell}\right)<\infty$, we can write $f=f^+-f^-$ where $f^+$ and $f^-$ are defined by 
$$f^+:=f\mathbf{1}_{\{f\ge0\}},~~~~f^-:=-f\mathbf{1}_{\{f<0\}}.$$
Similarly, we denote $f_m=f_m^+-f_m^-$, and define $S^+$, $S^-$, $S^+_m$ and $S_m^-$ by replacing $f$ in \eqref{def:ustat} by $f^+$, $f^-$, $f_m^+$ and $f_m^-$ respectively.
For $\ell\in\N$, by Minkowski inequality, 
\begin{eqnarray*}
    \E\left[\left|S-S_m\right|^{\ell}\right]&=&\E\left[\left|(S^+-S^-)-(S_m^+-S_m^-)\right|^{\ell}\right]\\
    &=&\E\left[\left|(S^+-S_m^+)-(S^--S_m^-)\right|^{\ell}\right]\\
    &\le&\E\left[\left|S^+-S_m^+\right|^{\ell}\right]+\E\left[\left|S^--S_m^-\right|^{\ell}\right],
\end{eqnarray*}
and it shows that $\lim_{m\to\infty}\E\left[\left|S-S_m\right|^{\ell}\right]=0$.
\end{enumerate}
Moreover, using Lemma~\ref{lem:limits_S_n}, we have 
\begin{equation}
\E[S^\ell]=\lim_{m\to\infty}\E[(S_m)^\ell]=\sum_{\pi\in\Pi^\ell(k)}\int_{\mathbb{X}^{|\pi|}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}(f^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathbb{Q}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}(\mathrm{d}\bm{u})\lambda^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x}).
\end{equation}
\end{proof}
\begin{proof}[Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:moments}]
\eqref{momeq-1} has already been provided in Lemma~\ref{lem:limits_S_n2}. 
%\eqref{momeq-2} and \eqref{momeq-3} are straightforward from \cite[Theorem~3.7]{schulte2}.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[1)]First, suppose that $f$ satisfies $\supp(f)\subseteq B^k\times\mathbb{M}^{k\times k}_{\ne}$ with $\lambda(B)<\infty$ and $\|f\|_{\infty}<\infty$. 
Then, by \cite[Theorem~3.7]{schulte2}, we have 
\begin{eqnarray*}
\E\left[(\widehat{S}^{(n)}-\E\widehat{S}^{(n)})^{\ell}\right]=\sum_{\pi\in\overline{\Pi}^\ell(k)}\int_{\widehat{\mathbb{X}}^{|\pi|}}(f^{\otimes \ell})_{\pi}(x_1,\dots,x_{|\pi|},\tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\dots,\hat{x}_{|\pi|}))\hat{\lambda}^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{\hat{x}}).
\end{eqnarray*}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:limits_S_n} and Lemma~\ref{lma1}, we have
\begin{eqnarray*}
\E\left[(S-\E S)^{\ell}\right]&=&\lim_{n\to\infty}\E\left[(\widehat{S}^{(n)}-\E\widehat{S}^{(n)})^{\ell}\right]\\
&=&\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{\pi\in\overline{\Pi}^\ell(k)}\int_{\widehat{\mathbb{X}}^{|\pi|}}(f^{\otimes \ell})_{\pi}(x_1,\dots,x_{|\pi|},\tilde{m}^{(n)}(\hat{x}_1,\dots,\hat{x}_{|\pi|}))\hat{\lambda}^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{\hat{x}})\\
&=&\sum_{\pi\in\overline{\Pi}^\ell(k)}\int_{\mathbb{X}^{|\pi|}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}(f^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathbb{Q}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}(\mathrm{d}\bm{u})\lambda^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x}).
\end{eqnarray*}
\item[2)]For general $f$ satisfying $\E\left(\overline{S}^\ell\right)<\infty$, let $f_m:\mathbb{X}^k\times \mathbb{M}^{k\times k}_{\neq}\to\mathbb{R}$ be defined by 
\begin{equation*}
f_m(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (u_{i,j})_{i,j\in[k], i\neq j}):=f(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (u_{i,j})_{i,j\in[k], i\neq j})\mathbf{1}_{\{(x_1,\hdots,x_k)\in B_m\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|f|\le m\}},
\end{equation*}
where $(B_m)_{m\in\mathbb{N}}$ is an increasing sequence of measurable sets such that $\lambda(B_m)<\infty$ for each $m\in\mathbb{N}$ and $ \cup_{m=1}^\infty B_m=\mathbb{X}$. Let $S_m$ be defined by replacing $f$ with $f_m$ in \eqref{def:ustat}. Because $|f_m|\le |f|$ and $f_m\to f$, by Lemma~\ref{lem:limits_S_n2} and dominated convergence theorem, we have 
\begin{eqnarray*}
    \E\left[(S-\E S)^{\ell}\right]&=&\lim_{m\to\infty}\E\left[(S_m-\E S_m)^{\ell}\right]\\
    &=&\lim_{m\to\infty}\sum_{\pi\in\overline{\Pi}^\ell(k)}\int_{\mathbb{X}^{|\pi|}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}(f_m^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathbb{Q}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}(\mathrm{d}\bm{u})\lambda^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x})\\
    &=&\sum_{\pi\in\overline{\Pi}^\ell(k)}\int_{\mathbb{X}^{|\pi|}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}(f^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathbb{Q}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}(\mathrm{d}\bm{u})\lambda^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x}).
\end{eqnarray*}
\end{enumerate}
We complete the proof of \eqref{momeq-2}. The cumulant identities \eqref{momeq-3} can be shown in a similar way.   
\end{proof}
%\section{Proofs}
\subsection{Proofs of Theorem~\ref{normalapp1} and Corollary~\ref{normalapp1k2}}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem \ref{normalapp1}]

\begin{enumerate}
\item[]
\item[1)] Assume $\supp(f)\subseteq B^k\times\mathbb{M}^{k\times k}_{\ne}$ with some measurable set $B$ satisfying $\lambda(B)$ and $\|f\|_{\infty}<\infty$. 
For $\widehat{S}^{(n)}$ given in \eqref{Ustat1}, applying Proposition~\ref{normalU-1}, it shows 
\begin{eqnarray}
	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{Wass}}\left(\frac{\widehat{S}^{(n)}-\E \widehat{S}^{(n)}}{\sqrt{\Var (\widehat{S}^{(n)})}},N\right)&\le&2k^{7/2}\sum_{1\le i\le j\le k}\frac{\sqrt{M_{ij}(f)}}{\Var (\widehat{S}^{(n)})},\\
	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{K}}\left(\frac{\widehat{S}^{(n)}-\E \widehat{S}^{(n)}}{\sqrt{\Var (\widehat{S}^{(n)})}},N\right)&\le&19k^5\sum_{i,j=1}^k\frac{\sqrt{M_{ij}(f)}}{\Var (\widehat{S}^{(n)})}
\end{eqnarray}
From Lemma~\ref{lem:limits_S_n}, we know that
\begin{equation}
\frac{\widehat{S}^{(n)}-\E \widehat{S}^{(n)}}{\sqrt{\Var (\widehat{S}^{(n)})}}\overset{L^2}{\longrightarrow}\frac{S-\E S}{\sqrt{\Var S}},
\end{equation}as $n\to\infty$.
Therefore, according to \cite[Lemma~6.2]{Last_Nestmann_Schulte}, we have
\begin{equation}
	\mathrm{d}_I\left(\frac{S-\E S}{\sqrt{\Var S}},N\right)\le\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathrm{d}_I\left(\frac{\widehat{S}^{(n)}-\E \widehat{S}^{(n)}}{\sqrt{\Var (\widehat{S}^{(n)})}},N\right),
\end{equation}
for $I\in\{\mathrm{Wass, K}\}$.

\item[2)] For general $f$, we can define $S_m$ be defined by replacing $f$ with $f_m$ in \eqref{def:ustat} with $f_m:\mathbb{X}^k\times \mathbb{M}^{k\times k}_{\neq}\to\mathbb{R}$ defined by 
\begin{equation*}
f_m(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (u_{i,j})_{i,j\in[k], i\neq j}):=f(x_1,\hdots,x_k, (u_{i,j})_{i,j\in[k], i\neq j})\mathbf{1}_{\{(x_1,\hdots,x_k)\in B_m\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|f|\le m\}},
\end{equation*}
for a sequence of measurable sets $(B_m)_{m\in\mathbb{N}}$ such that for each $m\in\mathbb{N}$, $\lambda(B_m)<\infty$ and $ B_m\uparrow\mathbb{X}$ as $m\to\infty$. 
From Lemma~\ref{lem:limits_S_n2}, we know that
\begin{equation}
\frac{S_m-\E S_m}{\sqrt{\Var S_m}}\overset{L^2}{\longrightarrow}\frac{S-\E S}{\sqrt{\Var S}},
\end{equation}
as $m\to\infty$. Again, using \cite[Lemma~6.2]{Last_Nestmann_Schulte}, we have
\begin{equation}
	\mathrm{d}_I\left(\frac{S-\E S}{\sqrt{\Var S}},N\right)\le\liminf_{m\to\infty}\mathrm{d}_I\left(\frac{S_m-\E S_m}{\sqrt{\Var S_m}},N\right),
\end{equation}
for $I\in\{\mathrm{Wass, K}\}$. By applying conclusion in part $1)$ to $S_m$, the proof is complete.
\end{enumerate}
