\begin{proof}[Proof of Corollary~\ref{normalapp1k2}]
In the following, we evaluate and bound the expressions $M_{11}(f)$, $M_{12}(f)$ and $M_{22}(f)$ from Theorem \ref{normalapp1}, which are sums of integrals associated with partitions. By symmetry of $f$, different partitions lead to the same integrals. For $M_{11}(f)$ one has 16 partitions of the form
$$
\big\{ \{1,3,5,7\}, \{2\}, \{4\}, \{6\}, \{8\} \big\}.
$$
For $M_{12}(f)$ one obtains the partitions
\begin{align*}
& \big\{ \{1,3,5,7\}, \{6,8\}, \{2\}, \{4\} \big\} \\
& \big\{ \{1,5,7\}, \{3,6,8\}, \{2\}, \{4\} \big\} \\
& \big\{ \{1,5\}, \{3,7\}, \{6,8\}, \{2\}, \{4\} \big\}
\end{align*}
with the multiplicities $16$, $16$ and $32$, while for $M_{22}(f)$ one has
\begin{align*}
& \big\{ \{2,3\}, \{4,5\}, \{6,7\}, \{8,1\} \big\} \\
& \big\{ \{1,5,7\}, \{2,3\}, \{4,6,8\} \big\} \\
& \big\{ \{1,3,5,7\}, \{2,4\}, \{6,8\} \big\} \\
& \big\{ \{1,3,5,7\}, \{2,4,6,8\} \big\}
\end{align*}
with the multiplicities $48$, $96$, $48$ and $8$. This leads to
\begin{align*}
M_{11}(f) & = 16\int_{\mathbb{X}^5} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{5\times 5}} \prod_{i=2}^5 |f(x_1,x_i,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,i\}})| \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{5\times 5}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^5(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}), \allowdisplaybreaks \\
M_{12}(f) & = 16\int_{\mathbb{X}^4} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{4\times 4}} |f(x_1,x_2,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,2\}}) f(x_1,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,3\}}) f(x_1,x_4,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,4\}})^2| \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{4\times 4}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^4(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}) \\
& \quad +16\int_{\mathbb{X}^4} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{4\times 4}} |f(x_1,x_2,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,2\}}) f(x_3,x_4,\mathbf{u}_{\{3,4\}}) f(x_1,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,3\}})^2| \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{4\times 4}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^4(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}) \\
& \quad +32\int_{\mathbb{X}^5} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{5\times 5}} |f(x_1,x_2,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,2\}}) f(x_3,x_4,\mathbf{u}_{\{3,4\}}) f(x_1,x_5,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,5\}})  f(x_3,x_5,\mathbf{u}_{\{3,5\}})| \\
& \hspace{3cm} \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{5\times 5}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^5(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}), \allowdisplaybreaks\\
M_{22}(f) & = 48\int_{\mathbb{X}^4} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{4\times 4}} |f(x_1,x_2,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,2\}}) f(x_2,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{2,3\}}) f(x_3,x_4,\mathbf{u}_{\{3,4\}}) f(x_4,x_1,\mathbf{u}_{\{4,1\}})| \\
& \hspace{3cm} \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{4\times 4}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^4(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}) \\
& \quad + 96 \int_{\mathbb{X}^3} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{3\times 3}} |f(x_1,x_2,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,2\}}) f(x_2,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{2,3\}}) f(x_1,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,3\}})^2| \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{3\times 3}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^3(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}) \\
& \quad + 48 \int_{\mathbb{X}^3} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{3\times 3}} f(x_1,x_2,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,2\}})^2 f(x_1,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{2,3\}})^2 \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{3\times 3}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^3(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}) \\
& \quad + 8 \int_{\mathbb{X}^2} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{2\times 2}} f(x_1,x_2,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,2\}})^4 \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{2\times 2}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^2(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}).
\end{align*}
Applying inequality $ab\leq \frac{a^2+b^2}{2}$ for $a,b\in(0,\infty)$ to the second summand of $M_{22}(f)$ leads to
\begin{align*}
& 96 \int_{\mathbb{X}^3} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{3\times 3}} |f(x_1,x_2,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,2\}}) f(x_2,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{2,3\}}) f(x_1,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,3\}})^2| \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{3\times 3}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^3(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}) \\
& \leq  48 \int_{\mathbb{X}^3} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{3\times 3}} (f(x_1,x_2,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,2\}})^2 + f(x_2,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{2,3\}})^2) f(x_1,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,3\}})^2 \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{3\times 3}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^3(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}) \\
& = 96 \int_{\mathbb{X}^3} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{3\times 3}} f(x_1,x_2,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,2\}})^2 f(x_1,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,3\}})^2 \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{3\times 3}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^3(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}),
\end{align*}
where the right-hand side is the third summand of $M_{22}(f)$. Similarly, for the first summand in $M_{22}(f)$ we obtain
\begin{align*}
& 48\int_{\mathbb{X}^4} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{4\times 4}} |f(x_1,x_2,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,2\}}) f(x_2,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{2,3\}}) f(x_3,x_4,\mathbf{u}_{\{3,4\}}) f(x_4,x_1,\mathbf{u}_{\{4,1\}})| \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{4\times 4}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^4(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}) \\
& \leq 24\int_{\mathbb{X}^4} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{4\times 4}} |f(x_1,x_2,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,2\}})  f(x_3,x_4,\mathbf{u}_{\{3,4\}}) (f(x_2,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{2,3\}})^2+f(x_4,x_1,\mathbf{u}_{\{4,1\}})^2)| \\
& \hspace{3cm} \times \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{4\times 4}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^4(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}) \\
& = 48\int_{\mathbb{X}^4} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{4\times 4}} |f(x_1,x_2,\mathbf{u}_{\{1,2\}})  f(x_3,x_4,\mathbf{u}_{\{3,4\}}) f(x_2,x_3,\mathbf{u}_{\{2,3\}})^2| \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{4\times 4}(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^4(\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}).
\end{align*}
Here the right-hand side is the second summand of $\widetilde{M}_{2}(f)$. The above inequalities yield that
$$
M_{22}(f) \leq 3 \widetilde{M}_2(f) + \widetilde{M}_3(f).
$$
Together with $M_{11}(f)=\widetilde{M}_1(f)$ and $M_{12}(f)=\widetilde{M}_2(f)$, the statement follows from Theorem \ref{normalapp1}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Proof of Theorem \ref{Poisson_process_approximation}}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem \ref{Poisson_process_approximation}]
The second inequality is an immediate consequence from Lemma \ref{lem:limits_S_n2} and \eqref{eqn:generalised_Mecke_U-statistics}, which yield that
\begin{align*}
& \E\xi(\mathbb{Y})^2 - (\E\xi(\mathbb{Y}))^2 - \E\xi(\mathbb{Y})  \\
& = \Var\xi(\mathbb{Y}) - \E\xi(\mathbb{Y})  \\
& = \sum_{\ell=1}^k \frac{\ell!}{(k!)^2} \binom{k}{\ell}^2 \int_{\mathbb{X}^{2k-\ell}} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{(2k-\ell)\times(2k-\ell)}} \mathbf{1}\{(x_1,\hdots,x_k,(\mathbf{u})_{i,j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j})\in\operatorname{dom} g\}  \\
& \hspace{4cm} \times \mathbf{1}\{(x_1,\hdots,x_\ell,x_{k+1},\hdots,x_{2k-\ell},(\mathbf{u})_{i,j})_{i,j\in[\ell]\cup\{k+1,\hdots,2k-\ell\}, i\neq j})\in\operatorname{dom} g\} \\
& \hspace{4cm} \times \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{(2k-\ell)\times (2k-\ell)}(d\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^{2k-\ell}(d(x_1,\hdots,x_{2k-\ell})) \\
& \quad - \frac{1}{k!} \int_{\mathbb{X}^k} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{k\times k}} \mathbf{1}\{(x_1,\hdots,x_k,(\mathbf{u})_{i,j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j})\in\operatorname{dom} g\} \, \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{k\times k}(d\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^{k}(d(x_1,\hdots,x_k)) \\
& = \sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1} \frac{\ell!}{(k!)^2} \binom{k}{\ell}^2 \int_{\mathbb{X}^{2k-\ell}} \int_{\mathbb{M}_{\neq}^{(2k-\ell)\times(2k-\ell)}} \mathbf{1}\{(x_1,\hdots,x_k,(\mathbf{u})_{i,j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j})\in\operatorname{dom} g\}  \\
& \hspace{4cm} \times \mathbf{1}\{(x_1,\hdots,x_\ell,x_{k+1},\hdots,x_{2k-\ell},(\mathbf{u})_{i,j})_{i,j\in[\ell]\cup\{k+1,\hdots,2k-\ell\}, i\neq j})\in\operatorname{dom} g\} \\
& \hspace{4cm} \times \mathbb{Q}_{\neq}^{(2k-\ell)\times (2k-\ell)}(d\mathbf{u}) \, \lambda^{2k-\ell}(d(x_1,\hdots,x_{2k-\ell})) \\
& \leq \frac{1}{k!} \sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1} \frac{1}{(k-\ell)!} \binom{k}{\ell} r(\operatorname{dom} g) \leq \frac{2^k}{k!} \sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1} r(\operatorname{dom} g).
\end{align*}

Next we show the first inequality for the case that there exists a set $B\subseteq \mathbb{X}$ such that $\operatorname{dom}' g\subseteq B^k$ and $\lambda(B)<\infty$. For $n\in\mathbb{N}$ let $\widetilde{\xi}^{(n)}$ and $\widehat{\xi}^{(n)}$ be as in Section \ref{Poisson_process_approximation} and recall that $\xi\overset{d}{=}\widetilde{\xi}^{(n)}$. The triangle inequality for the Kantorovich-Rubinstein distance yields that
$$
\mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\xi,\zeta)=\mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(n)},\zeta) \leq \mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(n)},\widehat{\xi}^{(n)}) + \mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\widehat{\xi}^{(n)},\zeta).
$$
From the definition of the Kantorovich-Rubinstein distance, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and \eqref{diff2} it follows that
\begin{align*}
	\mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(n)},\widehat{\xi}^{(n)}) & \leq \E\big[ \mathbf{1}\{ \widetilde{\xi}^{(n)}\neq\widehat{\xi}^{(n)} \} (\widetilde{\xi}^{(n)}(\mathbb{Y})+\widehat{\xi}^{(n)}(\mathbb{Y})) \big] \\
& \leq \mathbb{P}( \widetilde{\xi}^{(n)}\neq\widehat{\xi}^{(n)} )^{1/2} \big( (\E \widetilde{\xi}^{(n)}(\mathbb{Y})^2)^{1/2} + (\E \widetilde{\xi}^{(n)}(\mathbb{Y})^2)^{1/2} \big) \\
& \leq \varepsilon_n \sqrt{\sum_{\ell=1}^\infty \frac{1}{\ell^2} } \big( (\E \widetilde{\xi}^{(n)}(\mathbb{Y})^2)^{1/2} + (\E \widetilde{\xi}^{(n)}(\mathbb{Y})^2)^{1/2} \big).
\end{align*}
Because of $\E \widetilde{\xi}^{(n)}(\mathbb{Y})^2, \E \widetilde{\xi}^{(n)}(\mathbb{Y})^2 \leq \E \eta(B)^k<\infty$ by the assumption on $g$, we see that
$$
\lim_{n\to\infty} \mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\widetilde{\xi}^{(n)},\widehat{\xi}^{(n)})=0.
$$
From \cite[Theorem 3.1]{dst} we obtain that
$$
\mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\widehat{\xi}^{(n)},\zeta) \leq \mathrm{d}_{\rm TV}(\widehat{\mathbf{L}}^{(n)},\mathbf{M}) + 2 (\E \widehat{\xi}^{(n)}(\mathbb{Y})^2 - (\E \widehat{\xi}^{(n)}(\mathbb{Y}))^2 - \E \widehat{\xi}^{(n)}(\mathbb{Y}) ),
$$
where $\widehat{\mathbf{L}}^{(n)}$ is the intensity measure of $\widehat{\xi}^{(n)}$. Note that
$$
\widehat{\mathbf{L}}^{(n)} = \mathbf{L}.
$$
Combining this with Lemma \ref{lem:limits_S_n}, which is applicable as $\widehat{\xi}^{(n)}(\mathbb{Y})$ is a $U$-statistic, yields that
$$
\limsup_{n\to\infty} \mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\widehat{\xi}^{(n)},\zeta) \leq \mathrm{d}_{\rm TV}(\mathbf{L},\mathbf{M}) + 2 (\E \widehat{\xi}(\mathbb{Y})^2 - (\E \widehat{\xi}(\mathbb{Y}))^2 - \E \widehat{\xi}(\mathbb{Y}) ),
$$
which is the desired statement under the additional assumption on $\operatorname{dom}'g$.

Next we consider the situation of general $g$. Since $\lambda$ is $\sigma$-finite, there exists an increasing sequence $(B_m)_{m\in\mathbb{N}}$ such that $\lambda(B_m)<\infty$ for all $m\in\mathbb{N}$ and $\bigcup_{m=1}^\infty B_m=\mathbb{X}$. For $m\in\mathbb{N}$ we define
$$
\xi_m=\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{(x_1,\hdots,x_k)\in\eta^k_{\neq}\cap \operatorname{dom}'g \cap B_m^k} \delta_{f(x_1,\hdots,x_k,(\mathcal{M}_{i,j})_{i,j\in[k],i\neq j})}
$$
and denote by $\mathbf{L}_m$ the intensity measure of $\xi_m$. From the triangle inequality and the first part of the proof we know that
\begin{align*}
\mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\xi,\zeta) & \leq \mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\xi_m,\xi) + \mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\xi_m,\zeta) \\
			       & \leq  \mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\xi_m,\xi) + \mathrm{d}_{\rm TV}(\mathbf{L}_m,\mathbf{M}) + 2(\E\xi_m(\mathbb{Y})^2 - (\E\xi_m(\mathbb{Y}))^2 - \E\xi_m(\mathbb{Y}) )
\end{align*}
Because every point of $\xi_m$ is also contained in $\xi$, we deduce that
$$
\mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\xi_m,\xi) \leq \E[|\xi(\mathbb{Y})-\xi_m(\mathbb{Y})|] = \E[\xi(\mathbb{Y})] - \E[\xi_m(\mathbb{Y})].
$$
and
$$
\mathrm{d}_{\rm TV}(\mathbf{L}_m,\mathbf{L}) \leq \E[\xi(\mathbb{Y})] - \E[\xi_m(\mathbb{Y})].
$$
Together with the triangle inequality, the latter leads to
$$
\mathrm{d}_{\rm TV}(\mathbf{L}_m,\mathbf{M}) \leq \mathrm{d}_{\rm TV}(\mathbf{L}_m,\mathbf{L}) + \mathrm{d}_{\rm TV}(\mathbf{L},\mathbf{M}) \leq \mathrm{d}_{\rm TV}(\mathbf{L},\mathbf{M})  +  \E[\xi(\mathbb{Y})] - \E[\xi_m(\mathbb{Y})] ,
$$
so that
$$
\mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\xi,\zeta) \leq \mathrm{d}_{\rm TV}(\mathbf{L},\mathbf{M})  + 2 (\E[\xi(\mathbb{Y})] - \E[\xi_m(\mathbb{Y})]) + 2(\E\xi_m(\mathbb{Y})^2 - (\E\xi_m(\mathbb{Y}))^2 - \E\xi_m(\mathbb{Y}) ).
$$
Since, by the dominated convergence theorem, $\E\xi_m(\mathbb{Y})^2\to \E\xi(\mathbb{Y})^2$ and $\E\xi_m(\mathbb{Y})\to \E\xi(\mathbb{Y})$ as $m\to\infty$, we obtain
$$
\mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\xi,\zeta) \leq \mathrm{d}_{\rm TV}(\mathbf{L},\mathbf{M}) + 2(\E\xi(\mathbb{Y})^2 - (\E\xi(\mathbb{Y}))^2 - \E\xi(\mathbb{Y}) ),
$$
which completes the proof.
\end{proof}


\begin{proof}[Proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:Poisson_approximation}]
The statement follows from Theorem \ref{Poisson_process_approximation} exactly as Theorem 7.1 follows from Theorem 3.1 in \cite{dst}.
\end{proof}


\begin{proof}[Proof of Corollary~\ref{compound}]
The assertion can be deduced from Theorem \ref{Poisson_process_approximation} as Theorem 7.4 is derived from Theorem 3.1 in \cite{dst}.
\end{proof}
\subsection{Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:MDP}}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:MDP}]
	From \cite[Proposition~6.1]{schulte2}, we have 
	$$
	|\widetilde{\Pi}^\ell(k)|\le k^{k\ell}(\ell!)^k\le k^{3k(\ell-2)}(\ell!)^k.
	$$
	Together with Proposition~\ref{prop:moments}, we obtain 
\begin{align}
	\cum_\ell\left(\frac{S_n-\mathbb{E}S_n}{\sqrt{\Var S_n}}\right)
	&=\frac{\cum_\ell(S_n)}{(\Var S_n)^{\ell/2}}\nonumber\\
	&=(\Var S_n)^{-\ell/2}\sum_{\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^\ell(k)}\int_{\mathbb{X}^{|\pi|}}\int_{\mathbb{M}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}(f_n^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathbb{Q}^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}(\mathrm{d}\bm{u})\lambda_n^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x})\nonumber\\
	&\le (b_nk^{3k})^{\ell-2}(\ell!)^k.
\end{align}
The claim follows from the `main lemmas' presented in \cite{saulis}, see also the survey \cite{doering}, a result frequently refered to as the `Statulevičius condition'.
\end{proof}


\section{Proofs of applications}
\subsection{Proofs for subgraph counts}
 \begin{proof}[Proof of Proposition~\ref{subgraph-cumu-1}]
	Let $\mathbb{Q}$ be the uniform distribution on $[0,1]$. 
Define $f^{\subset}_G:(\R^d)^k\times [0,1]^{k(k-1)/2}\to\{0,1\}$ as 
\begin{equation}
	f^{\subset}_G(x_1,\dots,x_k,\mathbf{u}):=\frac1{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\mathbf{1}\{u_{i,j}\le\varphi(x_i-x_j)\},
\end{equation}
with $\mathbf{u}=(u_{i,j})_{1\le i<j\le k}$ and $u_{i,j}:=u_{j,i}$ if $i>j$. 
Similarly, we define 
$f^{=}_G:(\R^d)^k\times [0,1]^{k(k-1)/2}\to\{0,1\}$ as 
\begin{equation}
	f^{=}_G(x_1,\dots,x_k,\mathbf{u}):=\frac1{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\mathbf{1}\{u_{i,j}\le\varphi(x_i-x_j)\}\prod_{\{\imath,\jmath\}\notin E_G}\mathbf{1}\{u_{\imath,\jmath}>\varphi(x_{\imath}-x_{\jmath})\}.
\end{equation}
	Because of \eqref{momeq-3}, it is necessary to show that for each $\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^\ell(k)$
	\begin{equation}\label{intgrand-1}
		\int_{\Gamma_s^{|\pi|}}\int_{[0,1]^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}((f^{(i)}_G)^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathrm{d}\bm{u}\lambda_d^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x})\le C s
\end{equation}
For $n\in\mathbb{N}$, we use the abbreviation $\mathbf{I}_n:=\{(i,j)\in[n]^2:i<j\}$ and denote 
\begin{equation*}
	\mathcal{I}_n:=\{T\subset\mathbf{I}_n:|T|=n-1~\mbox{and the graph $([n],T)$ is connected}\}.
\end{equation*}
Because graph $G$ is connected, for any $\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^\ell(k)$, the contracted graph $(G^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}$ is still connected. 
Consequently, there exists a spanning tree of $(G^{\otimes \ell})_{\pi}$, which is isomorphic to a tree $I_{\pi}\in\mathcal{I}_{|\pi|}$.
Therefore, the integral in \eqref{intgrand-1} is bounded by 
\begin{align}
	&\int_{\Gamma_s^{|\pi|}}\int_{[0,1]^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}((f^{(i)}_G)^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathrm{d}\bm{u}\lambda_d^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x})\nonumber\\
	&\le\frac1{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^\ell}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^{|\pi|}}\mathbf{1}(x_1,\dots,x_{|\pi|}\in\Gamma_s)\left(\prod_{r=1}^\ell\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\varphi\left(x_i^{(r)}-x_j^{(r)}\right)\right)_{\pi}\gamma^{|\pi|}\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\nonumber\\
	&\le\frac1{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^\ell}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^{|\pi|}}\mathbf{1}(x_1\in\Gamma_s)\left(\prod_{r=1}^\ell\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\varphi\left(x_i^{(r)}-x_j^{(r)}\right)\right)_{\pi}\gamma^{|\pi|}\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\nonumber\\
	&\le\frac1{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^\ell}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^{|\pi|}}\mathbf{1}(x_1\in\Gamma_s)\prod_{\{i,j\}\in I_{|\pi|}}\varphi\left(x_i^{(r)}-x_j^{(r)}\right)_{\pi}\gamma^{|\pi|}\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\nonumber\\
&=\frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^\ell}\lambda_d(\Gamma_s)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varphi(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)^{|\pi|-1}\gamma^{|\pi|},\label{intgrand-2}
\end{align}
where the last equation is obtained by integrating successively beginning with the variables, except $x_1$, whose indices are leaves of the tree $I_{\pi}$.
Because $|\pi|\le \ell(k-1)+1$ for all $\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^\ell(k)$, combining \eqref{intgrand-2} with cumulant identities in \eqref{momeq-3}, we have 
\begin{align}
    	\cum_\ell(N_G^{I})&\le \sum_{\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^\ell(k)}\frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^\ell}\lambda_d(\Gamma_s)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varphi(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)^{|\pi|-1}\gamma^{|\pi|}\nonumber\\
        &\le \frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^\ell}\left[\left(\gamma \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varphi(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)\vee1\right]^{\ell(k-1)+1}\left|\widetilde{\Pi}^\ell(k)\right|s\nonumber\\
        &\le \frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^\ell}\left[\left(\gamma \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varphi(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)\vee1\right]^{\ell(k-1)+1}k^{k\ell}(\ell!)^ks,
\end{align}
where the last inequality comes from \cite[Proposition~6.1]{schulte2}. 
\end{proof}
\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{propgraph}]
% Because $f^{\subset}_G$ and $f^{=}_G$ are both non-negative and bounded by $\frac1{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|}$, 
% with \eqref{momeq-3}, we have, for $i\in\{\subset,=\}$, 
% \begin{align}
% &\Var(N_G^{(i)})\nonumber\\
% &=\sum_{\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^2(k)}\int_{\Gamma_s^{|\pi|}}\int_{[0,1]^{|\pi|(|\pi|-1)/2}}(f^{(i)}_G\otimes f^{(i)}_G)_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathrm{d}\bm{u}\gamma^{|\pi|}\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\label{graphvar0}\\
% &\le\frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^2}\sum_{\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^2(k)}\int_{\Gamma_s^{|\pi|}}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\varphi(x_i^{(1)}-x_j^{(1)})\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\varphi(x_i^{(2)}-x_j^{(2)})\right)_{\pi}(\bm{x})\gamma^{|\pi|}\mathrm{d}\bm{x}.\label{graphvar1}%\nonumber\\
% %	&=\frac{\gamma^{|\pi|}}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^2}\sum_{\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^2(k)}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^{|\pi|}}\mathbf{1}(x_1,\dots,x_{|\pi|}\in\Gamma_s)\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\varphi(x_i^{(1)}-x_j^{(1)})\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\varphi(x_i^{(2)}-x_j^{(2)})\right)_{\pi}(\bm{x})\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\nonumber
% \end{align}
% For $n\in\mathbb{N}$, we use the abbreviation $\mathbf{I}_n:=\{(i,j)\in[n]^2:i<j\}$ and denote 
% \begin{equation*}
% 	\mathcal{I}_n:=\{T\subset\mathbf{I}_n:|T|=n-1~\mbox{and the graph $([n],T)$ is connected}\}.
% \end{equation*}
% Because graph $G$ is connected, for any $\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^2(k)$, the graph with vertex set $\{x_i^{(1)},x_i^{(2)}:i\in[k]\}_{\pi}$ and edge set 
% $$\left\{\{x^{(1)}_i,x^{(1)}_j\},\{x^{(2)}_i,x^{(2)}_j\}:\{i,j\}\in E_G\right\}_{\pi}$$
% is a connected graph with $|\pi|$ vertices, denoted by $(G^{\otimes 2})_{|\pi|}$.
% Consequently, there exists a spanning tree of $(G^{\otimes 2})_{|\pi|}$, which is isomorphic to a tree $I_{|\pi|}\in\mathcal{I}_{|\pi|}$.
% Therefore, we have, from \eqref{graphvar1}
% \begin{align}
% \Var(N_G^{(i)})
% &\le\frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^2}\sum_{\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^2(k)}\int_{\Gamma_s^{|\pi|}}\left(\prod_{(i,j)\in I_{|\pi|}}\varphi(x_i-x_j)\right)(\bm{x})\gamma^{|\pi|}\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\nonumber\\
% &=\frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^2}\sum_{\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^2(k)}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^{r}}\mathbf{1}(x_1,\dots,x_{|\pi|}\in\Gamma_s)\left(\prod_{(i,j)\in I_{|\pi|}}\varphi(x_i-x_j)\right)(\bm{x})\gamma^{|\pi|}\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\nonumber\\
% &\le\frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^2}\sum_{\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^2(k)}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^{r}}\mathbf{1}(x_1\in\Gamma_s)\left(\prod_{(i,j)\in I_{|\pi|}}\varphi(x_i-x_j)\right)(\bm{x})\gamma^{|\pi|}\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\nonumber\\
% &=\frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^2}\sum_{\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^2(k)}\lambda_d(\Gamma_s)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varphi(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)^{|\pi|-1}\gamma^{|\pi|},\label{graphvar2}
% \end{align}
% where the last equation is obtained by integrating successively begining with the variables, except $x_1$, whose indices are leaves of the tree $I_{|\pi|}$.

When Assumption~\ref{graph-ass1}-ii) holds, it immediately implies that 
\begin{equation*}
	\mathbb{E}\sum_{\substack{(x_1,\dots,x_k)\in\eta^k_{\ne}\\ x_1\in[0,1]^d}}\left(\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\mathbf{1}(x_i\leftrightarrow x_j)\right)\left(\prod_{\{\imath,\jmath\}\notin E_G}\mathbf{1}(x_{\imath}\nleftrightarrow x_{\jmath})\right) >0.
\end{equation*}
Hence, it follows that
$$
\int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^{k-1}} \prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\varphi(x_i-x_j)\prod_{\{\imath,\jmath\}\notin E_G}(1-\varphi(x_{\imath}-x_{\jmath}))\mathrm{d}(x_2,\dots,x_k)>0.
$$
with $x_1:=0$. By the monotone convergence theorem there exists an $r_0\in(0,\infty)$ such that
$$
\underline{c}:=\inf_{x_1\in\Gamma}\int_{(B(x_1,r_0))^{k-1}} \prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\varphi(x_i-x_j)\prod_{\{\imath,\jmath\}\notin E_G}(1-\varphi(x_{\imath}-x_{\jmath}))\mathrm{d}(x_2,\dots,x_k)>0.
$$
Since $\Gamma$ is compact and convex and $\lambda_d(\Gamma)=1$, there exists an $s_0\geq 1$ such that
$$
\lambda_d(\{ y\in\Gamma_s: B(y,r_0)\subseteq\Gamma_s \})\geq \frac{s}{2},
$$
for all $s\ge s_0$.
Therefore, following from \eqref{momeq-3}, we have
\begin{align}
\Var(N_G^{=})
&=\sum_{\pi\in\widetilde{\Pi}^2(k)}\int_{\Gamma_s^{|\pi|}}\int_{[0,1]^{|\pi|(|\pi|-1)/2}}(f^{=}_G\otimes f^{=}_G)_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathrm{d}\bm{u}\gamma^{|\pi|}\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\nonumber\\
	&\ge\int_{(\Gamma_s)^{k}}\int_{[0,1]^{k(k-1)/2}}f^{=}_G(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathrm{d}\bm{u}\gamma^{k}\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\nonumber\\
	&=\frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|}\int_{(\Gamma_s)^k}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\varphi(x_i-x_j)\prod_{\{\imath,\jmath\}\notin E_G}(1-\varphi(x_{\imath}-x_{\jmath}))\gamma^k\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\nonumber\\
	&\ge\frac{\gamma^k}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|}\int_{\Gamma_s}\mathrm{d}x_1\int_{(\Gamma_s\cap B(x_1,r_0))^{k-1}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\varphi(x_i-x_j)\prod_{\{\imath,\jmath\}\notin E_G}(1-\varphi(x_{\imath}-x_{\jmath}))\mathrm{d}(x_2,\dots,x_k)\nonumber\\
	&\ge\frac{\underline{c}\gamma^k}{2|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|}s,\label{graphvar3}
\end{align} 
whenever $s\ge s_0$. 
%Therefore, by collecting \eqref{graphvar2} and \eqref{graphvar3}, we can show that 
%there exists contants $\underline{c}$, $C$, only depending on $\varphi$, $k$ and $G$ such that 
%\begin{equation}\label{varbound1}
%	\frac{\underline{c}\gamma^{k}}{2|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|}s\le \Var(N_G^{=})\le C' s.
%\end{equation}
On the other hand, using the estimates in \eqref{intgrand-0} with $\ell=4$, we have
\begin{equation}\label{mom-bound1}
	\max_{1\le i,j\le k}M_{i,j}(f^{=}_G)\le \tilde{C}s.
\end{equation}
Substituting $\eta$ in Theorem~\ref{normalapp1} with $\eta\cap\Gamma_s$, and $f$ with $f_G^{=}$, and collecting \eqref{graphvar3}, \eqref{mom-bound1} we obtain that
\begin{align}
	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{K}}\left(\frac{N_G^{=}-\mathbb{E} N_G^{=}}{\sqrt{\Var N_G^{=}}},N\right)&\le 19k^5\sum_{i,j=1}^k\frac{\sqrt{\tilde{C}s}}{\underline{c}\gamma^{k}s/(2|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|)}\le \frac{C}{\sqrt{s}}. 
\end{align}
The non-induced case can be treated analogously and is therefore omitted. This completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:subgraphMDP}]
	From Proposition~\ref{subgraph-cumu-1} and \eqref{graphvar3}, we obtain that for $\ell\ge3$,
\begin{align}
	\cum_{\ell}\left(\frac{N_n^{=}-\mathbb{E}N_n^{=}}{\sqrt{\Var(N_n^{=})}}\right)&=\frac{\cum_{\ell}(N_n^{=})}{\Var(N_n^{=})^{\ell/2}}\nonumber\\
																&\le\frac{
																\frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^\ell}\left[\left(\gamma \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varphi(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)\vee1\right]^{\ell(k-1)+1}k^{k\ell}(\ell!)^k s_n}{(\frac{\underline{c}\gamma^k}{2|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|}s_n)^{\ell/2}}\nonumber\\
																&=(\ell!)^ks_n^{1-\ell/2}T^\ell\left[\left(\gamma \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varphi(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)\vee1\right]\label{cumuboun1}
\end{align}
where 
$$
T:=k^k\left[\left(\gamma \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varphi(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)\vee1\right]^{k-1}\left(\frac2{\underline{c}\gamma^k|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|}\right)^{1/2}.
$$
Furthermore, since for $\ell\ge3$ we have
$$
\left[\left(\gamma \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varphi(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)\vee1\right]T^2 \le \left(\left[\left(\gamma \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varphi(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)\vee1\right]T^2\right)^{\ell-2},
$$
combining this with \eqref{cumuboun1} allows us to define
\begin{equation}\label{mdp-const1}
\Delta_n := \frac{\sqrt{s_n}}{\left[\left(\gamma \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varphi(z)\mathrm{d}z\right)\vee1\right]T^{3}}.
\end{equation}
According to Theorem~\ref{thm:MDP}, we complete the proof.
\end{proof}


\begin{proof}[Proof pf Lemma~\ref{noninteg-upper-1}]
	We need modifty the estimates appeared in \eqref{intgrand-2}. By letting $v_i:=s^{-1/d}x_i$, we have 
\begin{align}
  	&\int_{\Gamma_s^{|\pi|}}\int_{[0,1]^{|\pi|\times|\pi|}_{\ne}}((f^{\subset}_G)^{\otimes\ell})_{\pi}(\bm{x},\bm{u})\mathrm{d}\bm{u}\lambda_d^{|\pi|}(\mathrm{d}\bm{x})\nonumber\\
	&=\frac1{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^\ell}\int_{\Gamma_s^{|\pi|}}\left(\prod_{r=1}^\ell\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E_G}\varphi\left(x_i^{(r)}-x_j^{(r)}\right)\right)_{\pi}\gamma^{|\pi|}\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\nonumber\\
	&=\frac1{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^\ell}\int_{\Gamma_s^{|\pi|}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E((G^{\otimes\ell})_\pi)}\varphi(x_i-x_j)\gamma^{|\pi|}\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\nonumber\\
	&=\frac{\gamma^{|\pi|}}{|\mathrm{Aut}(G)|^\ell}\int_{\Gamma^{|\pi|}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E((G^{\otimes\ell})_\pi)}\varphi(s^{1/d}(v_i-v_j))s^{|\pi|}\mathrm{d}\bm{v}.\label{subgraph-integral-1}
\end{align}
Due to the Assumption~\ref{bound-ass-1} and the connection function $\varphi\le1$, we further have

\begin{align}\label{subgraph-integral-2}
	\int_{\Gamma^{|\pi|}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E((G^{\otimes\ell})_\ell)}\varphi(s^{1/d}(v_i-v_j))\mathrm{d}\bm{v}&\asymp s^{-e((G^{\otimes\ell})_\pi)\alpha/d}\int_{\Gamma^{|\pi|}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in E((G^{\otimes\ell})_\pi)}\varphi(v_i-v_j)\mathrm{d}\bm{v}.
\end{align}
Hence, \eqref{noninteg-upper-2} is obtained by combining estimates from \eqref{subgraph-integral-1} and \eqref{subgraph-integral-2}.
When $G$ is $K_1$-balanced, the dichotomy presented in \eqref{cumasym1} follows from  \eqref{noninteg-upper-2} and a convex analysis of the leading terms in the cumulant expressions \eqref{momeq-3}. This approach is analogous to the one employed in \cite[Proposition~7.1]{LiuPrivault25b}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Proofs for edge-lengths}

\subsubsection{Point process convergence for edge-lengths}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:edge-length_process}]
Let $A\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})$ be such that $A\subset [a,\infty)$. For the intensity measure $\widetilde{\mathbf{L}}_s$ of $\tilde{\xi}_s$ we obtain
\begin{align*}
\widetilde{\mathbf{L}}_s(A) & = \mathbb{E} \tilde{\xi}_s(A) = \frac{1}{2} \mathbb{E} \sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta_{\neq}^2} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{ x_1\leftrightarrow x_2,\frac{x_1+x_2}{2}\in\Gamma_s \bigg\} \mathbf{1}\{s^{-\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|x_1-x_2\| \in A\} \\
& = \frac{\gamma^2}{2} \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^2} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{ \frac{x_1+x_2}{2} \in \Gamma_s\bigg\} \mathbf{1}\{s^{-\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|x_1-x_2\|\in A\} \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_2\|) \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2) \\
& = \frac{\gamma^2}{2} \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^2} \mathbf{1}\{ y \in \Gamma_s\} \mathbf{1}\{s^{-\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|z\|\in A\} \bar{\varphi}(\|z\|) \, \mathrm{d}(y,z) \\
& = \frac{\gamma^2 \lambda_d(\Gamma_s)}{2} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \mathbf{1}\{s^{-\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|z\|\in A\} \bar{\varphi}(\|z\|) \, \mathrm{d}z = \frac{\gamma^2 s}{2} d\kappa_d \int_0^\infty \mathbf{1}\{s^{-\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}u\in A\} \bar{\varphi}(u) u^{d-1} \, \mathrm{d}u \\
& = \frac{d\kappa_d \gamma^2}{2} s^{1+\frac{d}{\alpha-d}} \int_0^\infty \mathbf{1}\{v\in A\} \bar{\varphi}(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}v) v^{d-1} \, \mathrm{d}v = \frac{d\kappa_d \gamma^2}{2} \int_A s^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-d}} \bar{\varphi}(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}v) v^{d-1} \, \mathrm{d}v.
\end{align*}
The intensity measure $\mathbf{L}$ of $\zeta^{(\alpha)}$ satisfies
\begin{align*}
\mathbf{L}(A) & = \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in\zeta} \mathbf{1}\{t^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}\in A\} = c_{\bar{\varphi}} \int_0^\infty \mathbf{1}\{t^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}\in A\} \, \mathrm{d}t \\
& = c_{\bar{\varphi}} (\alpha-d) \int_0^\infty \mathbf{1}\{v\in A\} v^{d-\alpha-1} \, \mathrm{d}v = \frac{\gamma^2 d \kappa_d }{2} \int_A l_{\bar{\varphi}}v^{d-\alpha-1} \, \mathrm{d}v.
\end{align*}
This implies
$$
|\widetilde{\mathbf{L}}_s(A)-\mathbf{L}(A)| \leq  \frac{\gamma^2 d \kappa_d }{2} \int_A |s^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-d}} \bar{\varphi}(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}v) v^{d-1}-l_{\bar{\varphi}}v^{d-\alpha-1}| \, \mathrm{d}v
$$
so that
\begin{align*}
\mathrm{d}_{\rm TV}(\widetilde{\mathbf{L}}_s|_A, \mathbf{L}|_A) & \leq \frac{\gamma^2 d \kappa_d }{2}\int_a^\infty |s^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-d}} \bar{\varphi}(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}v) v^{d-1}-l_{\bar{\varphi}}v^{d-\alpha-1}| \, \mathrm{d}v \\
& \leq \frac{\gamma^2 d \kappa_d }{2}\int_a^\infty \big|s^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-d}}v^{\alpha} \bar{\varphi}(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}v) -l_{\bar{\varphi}}\big| \, v^{d-\alpha-1}\, \mathrm{d}v.
\end{align*}
Moreover, we have
\begin{align*}
I_a & := \gamma^3 \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^3} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{ \frac{x_1+x_2}{2}, \frac{x_1+x_3}{2} \in \Gamma_s \bigg\} \mathbf{1}\{ \|x_1-x_2\|, \|x_1-x_3\| \geq a s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\} \\
& \hspace{4cm} \times \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_2\|) \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_3\|) \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2,x_3) \\
& \le \gamma^3 \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^3} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{ \frac{x_1+x_2}{2} \in \Gamma_s \bigg\} \mathbf{1}\{ \|x_1-x_2\|, \|x_1-x_3\| \geq a s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\} \\
& \hspace{4cm} \times \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_2\|) \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_3\|) \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2,x_3) \\
& \le \gamma^3 \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^3} \mathbf{1}\{ y \in \Gamma_s \} \mathbf{1}\{ \|z_1\|, \|z_2\| \geq a s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\} \bar{\varphi}(\|z_1\|) \bar{\varphi}(\|z_2\|) \, \mathrm{d}(y,z_1,z_2) \\
& = \gamma^3 \lambda_d(\Gamma_s) \bigg( \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \mathbf{1}\{\|z\|\geq a s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\} \bar{\varphi}(\|z\|) \, \mathrm{d}z \bigg)^2 = \gamma^3 s \bigg( d\kappa_d \int_{a s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}}^\infty \bar{\varphi}(u) u^{d-1}\, \mathrm{d}u \bigg)^2.
\end{align*}
From \eqref{eqn:Upper_bound_bar_varphi} it follows that
$$
\int_{a s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}}^\infty \bar{\varphi}(u) u^{d-1} \, \mathrm{d}u \leq C_{\bar{\varphi}} \int_{a s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}}^\infty u^{d-\alpha-1} \, \mathrm{d}u = \frac{C_{\bar{\varphi}}}{\alpha-d} (a s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}})^{d-\alpha} = \frac{C_{\bar{\varphi}}}{(\alpha-d) a^{\alpha-d} s}.
$$
This leads to
$$
I_a \leq \frac{\gamma^3 (d\kappa_d C_{\bar{\varphi}})^2}{(\alpha-d)^2 a^{2(\alpha-d)}} \frac{1}{s}.
$$
Now part a) follows from Theorem \ref{Poisson_process_approximation}.

By \eqref{eqn:Upper_bound_bar_varphi}, one has
$$
\big|s^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-d}}v^{\alpha} \bar{\varphi}(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}v) -l_{\bar{\varphi}}\big| v^{d-\alpha-1} \leq (C_{\bar{\varphi}}+l_{\bar{\varphi}}) v^{d-\alpha-1}
$$
for all $v\geq a$ and the right-hand side is integrable on $(a,\infty)$. Moreover, \eqref{eqn:assumption_phi} ensures that
$$
\lim_{s\to\infty} \big|s^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-d}}v^{\alpha} \bar{\varphi}(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}v) -l_{\bar{\varphi}}\big| \, v^{d-\alpha-1} = 0
$$
for all $v\geq a$. Thus, the dominated convergence theorem leads to
$$
\lim_{s\to\infty} \int_a^\infty \big|s^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-d}}v^{\alpha} \bar{\varphi}(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}v) -l_{\bar{\varphi}}\big| \, v^{d-\alpha-1}\, \mathrm{d}v = 0.
$$
Since the second part of the bound in part a) obviously vanishes as $s\to\infty$, we see that
$$
\lim_{s\to\infty} {\rm d}_{\rm KR}(\tilde{\xi}_s|_{[a,\infty)},\zeta^{(\alpha)}|_{[a,\infty)})=0.
$$
Since convergence in the Kantorovich--Rubinstein distance implies convergence in distribution, we obtain that
$$
\tilde{\xi}_s|_{[a,\infty)}\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}\zeta^{(\alpha)}|_{[a,\infty)} \quad \text{as} \quad s\to\infty.
$$
By \cite[Theorem 23.16]{Kallenberg_2023} this implies that $\tilde{\xi}_s\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}\zeta^{(\alpha)}$ as $s\to\infty$, which completes the proof of part b).

Let $a\in(0,\infty)$ be fixed. We see that
$$
\mathbb{P}(\zeta^{(\alpha)}((a,\infty))=0)= \mathbb{P}(\zeta((0,a^{d-\alpha}))=0) = e^{-c_{\bar{\varphi}} a^{d-\alpha}}=\mathbb{P}(Z>a)
$$
and that
$$
\mathbb{P}\bigg( s^{-\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\max_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta_{\neq}^2, x_1\leftrightarrow x_2, \frac{x_1+x_2}{2}\in\Gamma_s} \|x_1-x_2\|>a\bigg) = \mathbb{P}(\tilde{\xi}_s((a,\infty))=0).
$$
This implies that
\begin{align*}
\left| \mathbb{P}\bigg( s^{-\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\max_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta_{\neq}^2,x_1\leftrightarrow x_2, \frac{x_1+x_2}{2}\in\Gamma_s} \|x_1-x_2\|>a\bigg) - \mathbb{P}(Z>a) \right| & \leq {\rm d}_{\rm TV}(\tilde{\xi}_s((a,\infty)),\zeta^{(\alpha)}((a,\infty))) \\ & \leq {\rm d}_{\rm KR}(\tilde{\xi}_s|_{[a,\infty)},\zeta^{(\alpha)}|_{[a,\infty)}).
\end{align*}
Since, by part b), the right-hand side converges to zero as $s\to\infty$. This shows part c). 
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Asymptotic expectation and variance}
Proceeding as in \cite[Theorem 3.1]{reitznerschultethaele} gives the following formula for the expectation structure of $L_s(\tau)$ in \eqref{deflstau}. 

\begin{lemma} \label{lem:expectedlength}
    Assume that $\tau > -d$. It holds
    $$ \mathbb{E}[L_s (\tau)]= \frac{\gamma^2}{2} \int_0^{\operatorname{diam}(\Gamma_s)} \bar{\varphi} (r) r^{\tau + d -1} \int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
} f_s (r u) \, \mathrm{d}u \, \mathrm{d}r,$$
where $\operatorname{diam}(\Gamma_s)$ is the diameter of $\Gamma_s$,
$$f_s (ru) := \lambda_d \left( \Gamma_s \cap (\Gamma_s + ru) \right)$$
and $\mathrm{d}u$ stands for the infinitesimal element of the spherical Lebesgue measure. Moreover, it holds
\begin{align*}
    & \frac{\gamma^2}{2} d \kappa_d \lambda_d(\Gamma_s) \int_0^{\operatorname{diam}(\Gamma_s)} \bar{\varphi} (r) r^{\tau + d -1} \, \mathrm{d}r -\gamma^2\kappa_{d-1} \operatorname{surf}(\Gamma_s) \int_0^{\operatorname{diam}(\Gamma_s)} \bar{\varphi} (r) r^{\tau + d } \, \mathrm{d}r\\
    & \leq \mathbb{E}[L_s (\tau)] \leq \frac{\gamma^2}{2} d \kappa_d \lambda_d(\Gamma_s) \int_0^{\operatorname{diam}(\Gamma_s)} \bar{\varphi} (r) r^{\tau + d -1} \, \mathrm{d}r,
\end{align*}
where $\kappa_d$ denotes the volume of the $d$-dimensional unit ball and $\operatorname{surf}(\Gamma_s)$ denotes the surface area of the set  $\Gamma_s$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By Proposition \ref{prop:expectation_variance} a) we have
\begin{align*}
\mathbb{E}[L_s (\tau)]
  &= \frac{\gamma^2}{2} \int_{\Gamma_s} \int_{\Gamma_s} \mathbf{1}\{\|x-y\|\le\operatorname{diam}(\Gamma_s)\}
       \varphi(x-y)\,\|y-x\|^{\tau}\, \mathrm{d}x\, \mathrm{d}y \\[0.5em]
  &= \frac{\gamma^2}{2}  \int_{\Gamma_s} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \mathbf{1}\{\|z\|\le\operatorname{diam}(\Gamma_s)\}
       \varphi(z)\,\|z\|^{\tau}\, \mathbf{1}_{\{y+z \in \Gamma_s\}}\, \mathrm{d}z\, \mathrm{d}y \\[0.5em]
  &= \frac{\gamma^2}{2} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \mathbf{1}\{\|z\|\le\operatorname{diam}(\Gamma_s)\} \varphi(z)\,\|z\|^{\tau}\,
       \lambda_d\big(\Gamma_s\cap (\Gamma_s+z)\big)\, \mathrm{d}z \\[0.5em]
  &= \int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}} 
      \mathbf{1}\{r\le\operatorname{diam}(\Gamma_s)\} \bar{\varphi}(r)\,r^{\tau+d-1}\,
       \lambda_d\!\big(\Gamma_s\cap (\Gamma_s+ru)\big)\,
        \mathrm{d}u\, \mathrm{d}r \\
  &= \frac{\gamma^2}{2} \int_{0}^{\operatorname{diam}(\Gamma_s)}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}} 
       \bar{\varphi}(r)\,r^{\tau+d-1}\,
       \lambda_d\!\big(\Gamma_s\cap (\Gamma_s+ru)\big)\,
        \mathrm{d}u\, \mathrm{d}r.
\end{align*}
Now the proof is completed by applying Cauchy’s surface area formula.
\end{proof}



Note that Lemma \ref{lem:expectedlength} shows that the asymptotics of the expectation of power-weighted edge-lengths depends on whether the integral
$$\int_0^\infty \bar{\varphi} (r) r^{\tau + d -1} \, \mathrm{d} r $$
is finite or not. If we assume that $\bar{\varphi}$ has polynomial decay $\bar{\varphi}(r) \sim r^{-\alpha}$, $r \to \infty$, for some exponent $\alpha$, this translates to whether $\alpha>\tau +d$ or $\alpha\leq\tau +d$.

Next we give a formula for the variance.

\begin{lemma} \label{lem:varlength}
    Let $\tau > -d/2$. Then $L_s (\tau)$ has finite variance given by
    \begin{align}\label{varformula}
    \begin{split}
        & \operatorname{Var}[L_s (\tau)] = \frac{\gamma^2}{2} \int_{\Gamma_s}\int_{\Gamma_s} \varphi(x-y) \| y-x \|^{2\tau} \, \mathrm{d}x \, \mathrm{d}y  \\
        & \quad + \gamma^3\int_{\Gamma_s}\int_{\Gamma_s} \varphi(x_1-y) \| y-x_1 \|^\tau \, \mathrm{d} x_1 \int_{\Gamma_s} \varphi(x_2-y)  \| y-x_2 \|^\tau \, \mathrm{d} x_2 \, \mathrm{d} y.
    \end{split} 
    \end{align}
Moreover, if $\bar{\varphi}$ satisfies Assumption \ref{edgelength-ass1}, there is a constant $C \in (0, \infty)$ independent of $s$ such that
$$ \frac{1}{C} s \left( \frac{1}{C} + s^{\frac{2\tau + d -\alpha}{d}} \right) \leq \operatorname{Var}[L_s (\tau)] \leq Cs\left( {C} + s^{\frac{2\tau + d -\alpha}{d}} \right), \quad \text{ if } \alpha \neq d+2\tau,$$
and
$$ \frac{1}{C} s \left( \frac{1}{C} + \log s^{\frac{1}{d}} \right) \leq \operatorname{Var}[L_s (\tau)] \leq  Cs ( C +\log s^{\frac{1}{d}}), \quad \text{ if } \alpha = d+2\tau.$$
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
    The formula in \eqref{varformula} follows from Proposition \ref{prop:expectation_variance} b). %can be proven by proceeding as in the proof of \cite[Theorem 3.3]{reitznerschultethaele}. 
    Based on this formula, we now calculate the order of the variance. Concerning the first term on the right-hand side of \eqref{varformula}, similarly as in the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:expectedlength} we have
    
\begin{align*}
I(s) 
  &:= \int_{\Gamma_s} \int_{\Gamma_s} 
       \varphi(x-y)\,\|y-x\|^{2\tau}\, \mathrm{d}x\, \mathrm{d}y \\[0.5em]
  &= \int_{\Gamma_s} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} 
       \varphi(z)\,\|z\|^{2\tau}\, \mathbf{1}_{\{y+z \in \Gamma_s\}}\, \mathrm{d}z\, \mathrm{d}y \\[0.5em]
  &= \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \varphi(z)\,\|z\|^{2\tau}\,
       \lambda_d\big(\Gamma_s\cap (\Gamma_s+z)\big)\, \mathrm{d}z \\[0.5em]
  &= \int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}} 
       \bar{\varphi}(r)\,r^{2\tau+d-1}\,
       \lambda_d\!\big(\Gamma_s\cap (\Gamma_s+ru)\big)\,
        \mathrm{d}u\, \mathrm{d}r \\
  &= \int_{0}^{cs^{\frac{1}{d}}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}} 
       \bar{\varphi}(r)\,r^{2\tau+d-1}\,
       \lambda_d\!\big(\Gamma_s\cap (\Gamma_s+ru)\big)\,
        \mathrm{d}u\, \mathrm{d}r
\end{align*}
with
\begin{align*}
   d \kappa_d \lambda_d(\Gamma_s)  -\kappa_{d-1} \operatorname{surf}(\Gamma_s) r\leq \int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}} 
       \lambda_d\!\big(\Gamma_s\cap (\Gamma_s+ru)\big)\,
        \mathrm{d}u \leq d \kappa_d \lambda_d(\Gamma_s) 
\end{align*}
so that the variance asymptotics depends on whether
$$\int_0^\infty \bar{\varphi} (r) r^{2\tau + d -1} \mathrm{d} r< \infty$$ 
or not. Moreover the asymptotics of $I(s)$, as $s \to \infty$, is
$$ C_1 s (C_1 + s^{\frac{2\tau + d -\alpha}{d}} )\leq I(s) \leq C_2 s (C_2 + s^{\frac{2\tau + d -\alpha}{d}} ) $$
for some constants $C_1, C_2>0$, if $\alpha \neq 2\tau +d$, and
$$ C_1 s (C_1 + \log s^{\frac{1}{d}} )\leq I(s) \leq C_2 s (C_2 + \log s^{\frac{1}{d}} ) $$
if $\alpha = 2\tau +d$.

Similarly, considering the second term on the right-hand side of \eqref{varformula} we obtain
\begin{align*}
J(s) 
  &:= \int_{\Gamma_s}\!
     \Bigg(\int_{\Gamma_s} \varphi(x_1-y)\,\|y-x_1\|^{\tau}\,\mathrm{d}x_1\Bigg)
     \Bigg(\int_{\Gamma_s} \varphi(x_2-y)\,\|y-x_2\|^{\tau}\,\mathrm{d}x_2\Bigg)
     \mathrm{d}y \\[0.4em]
  &= \int_{\Gamma_s}\!
     \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^d}
        \varphi(z_1)g(z_2)\,\|z_1\|^{\tau}\|z_2\|^{\tau}\,
        \mathbf{1}_{\{y+z_1\in \Gamma_s\}}\,
        \mathbf{1}_{\{y+z_2\in \Gamma_s\}}\,
        \mathrm{d}z_1\,\mathrm{d}z_2\,\mathrm{d}y \\[0.4em]
  &= \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^d}
        \varphi(z_1)\varphi(z_2)\,\|z_1\|^{\tau}\|z_2\|^{\tau}\,
        \lambda_d\!\big(\Gamma_s\cap(\Gamma_s+z_1)\cap(\Gamma_s+z_2)\big)\,
        \mathrm{d}z_1\,\mathrm{d}z_2 \\[0.6em]
  &= \int_{0}^{cs^{\frac{1}{d}}}\!\!\int_{0}^{cs^{\frac{1}{d}}}
     \int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\!\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}
        \varphi(r_1)\varphi(r_2)\,r_1^{\tau}r_2^{\tau}\,
      \lambda_d\!\big(\Gamma_s\cap(\Gamma_s+r u_1)\cap(\Gamma_s+r u_2)\big)\,
        \mathrm{d}u_1\,\mathrm{d}u_2\,
        \mathrm{d}r_1\,\mathrm{d}r_2 
\end{align*}
with the asymptotic bound
$$ \frac{1}{C} s\leq \lambda_d\!\big(\Gamma_s\cap(\Gamma_s+r u_1)\cap(\Gamma_s+r u_2)\big) \leq {C} s ,$$
so that the assertion follows easily.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Regimes with central limit theorem} \label{regimeclt}

The next statement provides a quantitative central limit theorem in partial regimes for the exponent $\alpha$. Throughout we will suppose that Assumption \ref{edgelength-ass1} is satisfied.

\begin{theorem} \label{CLT:sumsedgelengths}
    Let $\tau > -d/2$. Assume that $\alpha \in (2\tau + d, \infty)$ or that $\alpha \in (d,2d)$ (with $\alpha \neq 2\tau + d$). Then there is a constant $C \in (0, \infty)$ independent of $s$ such that
    \begin{align*}
	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{Wass}}\left(\frac{L_s(\tau)-\E L_s(\tau)}{\sqrt{\Var L_s(\tau)}},N\right) & \le C \max\left\{ s^{-\frac{1}{2}} , s^{\frac{4\tau -{\alpha}}{2d}}  \right\},\\
	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{K}}\left(\frac{L_s(\tau)-\E L_s(\tau)}{\sqrt{\Var L_s(\tau)}},N\right) & \le  C \max\left\{ s^{-\frac{1}{2}} , s^{\frac{4\tau -{\alpha}}{2d}}\right\},
\end{align*}
    if $\alpha\in (2\tau + d, \infty)$ or
     \begin{align*}
	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{Wass}}\left(\frac{L_s(\tau)-\E L_s(\tau)}{\sqrt{\Var L_s(\tau)}},N\right) & \le C \max\{ s^{-\frac{1}{2}},  s^{-\frac{\tau}{2d}} ,  s^{-\frac{\alpha}{d}}, s^{\frac{\alpha}{2d}-1} \},\\
	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{K}}\left(\frac{L_s(\tau)-\E L_s(\tau)}{\sqrt{\Var L_s(\tau)}},N\right) & \le  C \max\{ s^{-\frac{1}{2}},  s^{-\frac{\tau}{2d}} ,  s^{-\frac{\alpha}{d}}, s^{\frac{\alpha}{2d}-1} \} ,
    \end{align*}
    if $\alpha \in (d,2d)$ and $\alpha < 2\tau + d$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
    The theorem will be a consequence of Corollary \ref{normalapp1k2}. First assume that $\alpha \in (2\tau + d, \infty)$. In this proof we denote by $c$ an unspecified positive constant that might change from line to line. We can estimate each expression $\widetilde{M}_i(f)$, $i \in \{1,2,3 \}$, in the latter corollary as follows. Considering the term $\widetilde{M}_1(f)$ we have
    \begin{align*}
\widetilde{M}_1(f)
&= c \int_{\Gamma_s^5} \prod_{j=2}^{5} \|x_1-x_j\|^{\tau}\,\varphi(x_1-x_j)\,\mathrm{d}x_1\cdots \mathrm{d}x_5 \\[4pt]
&= c\int_{\Gamma_s} \left( \int_{\Gamma_s^4} \prod_{j=2}^{5}  \|x_1-x_j \|^{\tau}\,\varphi(x_1-x_j)\,\mathrm{d}x_2\cdots \mathrm{d}x_5 \right) \mathrm{d}x_1 \\[4pt]
&= \int_{\Gamma_s} \left( \int_{\Gamma_s(-x_1)} |u|^{\tau}\,\varphi(u)\,\mathrm{d}u \right)^{4} \mathrm{d}x_1,
\end{align*}
where $\Gamma_s(-x_1) \;:=\; \{\, u \in \mathbb{R}^d : u + x_1 \in \Gamma_s \,\}$ and we used the substitution $u=x_1-x_j$ for each $j\ge 2$. S\text{ince }$\Gamma_s(-x_1)\subseteq \Gamma_{cs}$ \text{ for }$x_1\in\Gamma_s$, the latter can be further estimated by
\begin{align*}
&\int_{\Gamma_s} \left( \int_{\Gamma_s(-x_1)} |u|^{\tau}\,\varphi(u)\,\mathrm{d}u \right)^{4} \mathrm{d}x_1 \leq \lambda_d\big(\Gamma_s\big)\,\left( \int_{\Gamma_{cs}} |u|^{\tau}\,\varphi(u)\,\mathrm{d}u \right)^{4}
\end{align*}
\text{Assuming }$\alpha>2\tau+d$ we have
\begin{align*}
\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} |u|^{\tau}\varphi(u)\,\mathrm{d}u =:\kappa<\infty.
\end{align*}
Consequently
$$\int_{\Gamma_{cs}} |u|^{\tau}\varphi(u)\,\mathrm{d}u \leq \kappa,$$
and therefore
\begin{align} \label{cltedgelengthsm11}
\widetilde{M}_1(f) \leq  c\lambda_d\big(\Gamma_s\big)\,\kappa^{4}
\;=\; C\,s\qquad(\text{for some }C>0).
\end{align}

%Proposal Matthias:
%$$
%M_{11}(f) \leq \rho^d \max\{1,\rho^{\tau+d-\alpha}\}^4
%$$


Next we consider $\widetilde{M}_2(f)$ in the statement of Corollary \ref{normalapp1k2}. The first term in the definition of this quantity is
\begin{align*}
    & c \int_{\Gamma_s}  \|x_1-x_2\|^{\tau}\,\varphi(x_1-x_2)  \|x_1-x_3\|^{\tau}\,\varphi(x_1-x_3) \|x_1-x_4\|^{2\tau}\,\varphi(x_1-x_4) \,\mathrm{d}x_1\cdots \mathrm{d}x_4 .
\end{align*}
Proceeding as before we obtain that the last expression can be estimated by
$$ c s \left( (c + c s^{\frac{\tau + d -\alpha}{d}})^2 (c + c s^{\frac{2\tau + d -\alpha}{d}}) \right) .$$
The other terms in the definition of $\widetilde{M}_2(f)$ are handled similarly. Using the fact $\alpha>2\tau+d$ we thus obtain
\begin{align} \label{cltedgelengthsm12}
\widetilde{M}_2(f) \leq  c s .
\end{align}
Using similar observations, one can see that
\begin{align} \label{cltedgelengthsm13}
\begin{split}
& \widetilde{M}_3(f) \\
&\leq (c + c s^{\frac{\tau + d -\alpha}{d}})^4 + (c + c s^{\frac{\tau + d -\alpha}{d}}) (c+cs^{\frac{2\tau + d -\alpha}{d}}) + c s (c + c s^{\frac{2\tau + d -\alpha}{d}})^2 + c s (c + c s^{\frac{4\tau + d -\alpha}{d}}).
\end{split}
\end{align}
%Proposal Matthias for the first two terms
%$$
%\rho^d \max\{1,\rho^{\tau+d-\alpha}\}^2 \max\{1,\rho^{2\tau+d-\alpha}\} + \rho^d \max\{1,\rho^{\tau+d-\alpha}\} \max\{1,\rho^{2\tau+d-\alpha}\} 
%$$
%$$
%\rho^d \max\{1,\rho^{\tau+d-\alpha}\}^2 \max\{1,\rho^{2\tau+d-\alpha}\} \leq \rho^d \max\{1,\rho^{2\tau+d-\alpha}\}^2 \leq  \rho^d \max\{1,\rho^{4\tau+d-\alpha}\}
%$$
%\begin{align*}
%& \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau \|x_2-x_3\|^\tau \|x_3-x_4\|^\tau \|x_4-x_1\|^\tau \\
%& \leq \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau \|x_2-x_3\|^\tau \|x_3-x_4\|^{2\tau} + \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau \|x_2-x_3\|^\tau \|x_4-x_1\|^{2\tau}
%\end{align*}
Combining \eqref{cltedgelengthsm11}, \eqref{cltedgelengthsm12}, \eqref{cltedgelengthsm13} with Lemma \ref{lem:varlength} and Corollary \ref{normalapp1k2} yields the statement in the theorem. The case $\alpha\in (d,2d)$ with $\alpha < 2\tau +d$ follows from the same arguments, and by noting that the order of the variance is different in this case.
\end{proof}

%\begin{remark}
%    If $\alpha \in (d,2d)$ the above proof together with Lemma \ref{lem:varlength} implies that $L_s(\tau)$ satisfies a CLT as well with
%    \begin{align*}
%	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{Wass}}\left(\frac{L_s(\tau)-\E L_s(\tau)}{\sqrt{\Var L_s(\tau)}},N\right) & \le C \max\{ s^{-\frac{1}{2}},  s^{-\frac{\tau}{2d}} ,  s^{-\frac{\alpha}{d}}, s^{\frac{\alpha}{2d}-1} \},\\
%	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{K}}\left(\frac{L_s(\tau)-\E L_s(\tau)}{\sqrt{\Var L_s(\tau)}},N\right) & \le  C \max\{ s^{-\frac{1}{2}},  s^{-\frac{\tau}{2d}} ,  s^{-\frac{\alpha}{d}}, s^{\frac{\alpha}{2d}-1} \} .
%\end{align*}
%\end{remark}

Note that the first statement in Theorem \ref{CLT:sumsedgelengths} only yields a central limit theorem in partial regimes for the value of $\alpha$. In order to obtain it for the full regime, albeit without quantitative convergence rates, we use the following truncation technique.

For $a>0$ define
$$
L_{s,a} (\tau):=\frac{1}{2} \sum_{(x_1, x_2) \in \eta^2_{\neq} \cap \Gamma_s^2} \mathbf{1}\{ x_1 \leftrightarrow x_2, \|x_1-x_2\|\leq a \} \| x_1- x_2 \|^\tau.
$$
We aim to estimate
$$
\frac{\mathbb{E}[(L_{s,a} (\tau)-L_{s} (\tau))^2]}{s}. %\leq c a^{-...}
$$
By definition we have
\begin{align*}
L_{s,a}(\tau)-L_{s}(\tau)
&= \frac{1}{2}\sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}\cap \Gamma_s^2}
\mathbf{1}\{x_1\leftrightarrow x_2\}
\|x_1-x_2\|^{\tau}
\big(\mathbf{1}_{\{\|x_1-x_2\|\le a\}}-1\big) \\[0.4em]
&= -\frac{1}{2}\sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}\cap \Gamma_s^2}
\mathbf{1}\{x_1\leftrightarrow x_2,\ \|x_1-x_2\|>a\}\,
\|x_1-x_2\|^{\tau},
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
& (L_{s,a}(\tau)-L_{s}(\tau))^2\\
& = \frac{1}{4}\!\!\sum_{(x_1,x_2),(x_3,x_4)\in\eta^2_{\neq}\cap \Gamma_s^4}
\!\!\!\!\mathbf{1}\{x_1\leftrightarrow x_2,\|x_1-x_2\|>a\}
\mathbf{1}\{x_3\leftrightarrow x_4,\|x_3-x_4\|>a\}
\|x_1-x_2\|^{\tau}\|x_3-x_4\|^{\tau},
\end{align*}
so that
\begin{align*}
\mathbb{E}\!\left[(L_{s,a}(\tau)-L_{s}(\tau))^2\right]
&= C_1 I_a(s) + C_2 J_a(s),
\end{align*}
where, by Mecke’s formula,
\begin{align*}
I_a(s)
&= \int_{\Gamma_s}\!\!\int_{\Gamma_s}
\mathbf{1}_{\{\|x_1-x_2\|>a\}}\,
\varphi(x_1-x_2)\,\|x_1-x_2\|^{2\tau}\,\mathrm{d}x_1\,\mathrm{d}x_2, \\[0.3em]
J_a(s)
&= \int_{\Gamma_s}\!
\Bigg(\int_{\Gamma_s}
\mathbf{1}_{\{\|x_1-x_2\|>a\}}\,
\varphi(x_1-x_2)\,\|x_1-x-2\|^{\tau}\,\mathrm{d}x_1\Bigg)^{\!2}\mathrm{d}x_2.
\end{align*}
Assuming $\varphi(r)\sim C r^{-\alpha}$ with $\alpha>2\tau+d$, the tails are integrable and the same calculations as before show that
\begin{align*}
I_a(s),\,J_a(s)\;\leq \;cs\,a^{2\tau+d-\alpha},
\end{align*}
so that we have proven the following.

\begin{lemma}\label{truncationlemma1}
    There exists a constant $c \in (0,\infty)$ not depending on $a$ such that
    $$\frac{\mathbb{E}[(L_{s,a} (\tau)-L_{s} (\tau))^2]}{s} \leq c a^{2\tau+d-\alpha} .$$
\end{lemma}

Next we prove that $L_{s,a} (\tau)$ satisfies a central limit theorem for every $a>0$ and the regime $2\tau+d<\alpha$. The strategy is identical to the proof of Theorem \ref{CLT:sumsedgelengths}, with the key difference that the truncation will give us better (quantitative convergence) bounds.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:CLT_truncated}
Let $\tau > -d$ and $a>0$. 
Assume that $\bar{\varphi}$ satisfies Assumption \ref{edgelength-ass1} with exponent $\alpha>2\tau+d$. Then there exists a constant $C\in(0,\infty)$, independent of $s$, such that
\begin{align*}
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{Wass}}\!\left(
\frac{L_{s,a}(\tau)-\E L_{s,a}(\tau)}{\sqrt{\Var L_{s,a}(\tau)}},
N
\right)
&\le C\,s^{-1/2},\\[0.4em]
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{K}}\!\left(
\frac{L_{s,a}(\tau)-\E L_{s,a}(\tau)}{\sqrt{\Var L_{s,a}(\tau)}},
N
\right)
&\le C\,s^{-1/2}.
\end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The proof follows the same steps as in Theorem~\ref{CLT:sumsedgelengths}, 
with the additional indicator $\mathbf{1}_{\{\|x_1-x_2\|\le a\}}$ restricting all integrals to a bounded region. 
For instance,
\[
\widetilde{M}_1(f_a)
= c \int_{\Gamma_s}
\!\left(
\int_{\Gamma_s(-x_1)\cap B(0,a)} |u|^{\tau}\varphi(u)\,\mathrm{d}u
\right)^{\!4}
\mathrm{d}x_1
\le C\,s,
\]
since $\int_{B(0,a)}|u|^{\tau}\varphi(u)\,\mathrm{d}u<\infty$.
Analogous bounds hold for $\widetilde{M}_2(f_a)$ and $\widetilde{M}_3(f_a)$.
Thus, all moment terms in Corollary~\ref{normalapp1k2} are of order $s$, 
and by Lemma~\ref{lem:varlength} the variance satisfies $\Var[L_{s,a}(\tau)]\geq cs$.
Applying Corollary~\ref{normalapp1k2} yields the stated bounds with rate $s^{-1/2}$.
\end{proof}

Thus, Lemma \ref{truncationlemma1} and Lemma \ref{lem:CLT_truncated} yield the following theorem, giving a central limit theorem in the entire regime $\alpha>2\tau+d$ for $L_{s}(\tau)$.

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:CLTedgelengths_full}
Let $\tau > -d$ and assume that $\bar{\varphi}(r)$ satisfies Assumption \ref{edgelength-ass1} with exponent $\alpha>2\tau+d$. 
Then, as $s\to\infty$,
\[
\frac{L_{s}(\tau)-\E[L_{s}(\tau)]}{\sqrt{\Var[L_{s}(\tau)]}}
\overset{d}{\longrightarrow} N.
\]
\end{theorem}


\subsubsection*{The critical case \texorpdfstring{$\alpha = 2\tau + d$}{α = 2τ + d}}
\label{subsubsec:criticalcase}

We now turn to the borderline situation in which the decay exponent of $\bar{\varphi}$ satisfies
$\alpha = 2\tau + d$. Recall that $\Gamma_s \subset \mathbb{R}^d$ is a convex, compact observation
window with Lebesgue measure $\lambda_d(\Gamma_s)=s$. Let us assume that $\tau >0$. In the critical case we have
\[
|u|^{\tau}\,\bar{\varphi}(u) \;\leq \; c\min\{ 1,|u|^{-(\tau + d)}\}, 
\]
and hence
\[
\int_{\Gamma_{cs}} |u|^{\tau}\,\bar{\varphi}(u)\,\mathrm{d}u
\;\leq\;
c+c\int_1^{{cs}} r^{\tau}r^{-2\tau - d}r^{d-1}\,\mathrm{d}r
\; \leq c\, + cs^{-\frac{\tau}{d}},
\]
for some constant $c>0$ not depending on $s$. Inserting this estimate into the bound for $\widetilde{M}_1(f)$ from~\eqref{cltedgelengthsm11} gives
\[
\widetilde{M}_1(f)
\;\leq\;
 c \lambda_d(\Gamma_s)\,\Big(\int_{\Gamma_{cs}} |u|^{\tau}\bar{\varphi}(u)\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{4}
\;\leq\;
s\,(c+cs^{-\frac{\tau}{d}})^4.
\]
Analogous considerations show that for $\widetilde{M}_2(f)$ appearing in
Corollary~\ref{normalapp1k2} we have
\begin{align*}
   \widetilde{M}_2(f)\leq s (c +s^{-\frac{\tau}{d}})^2(c + \log s^{\frac{1}{d}}) + s(c+cs^{-\frac{\tau}{d}})^4
\end{align*}
and, moreover, for the term $\widetilde{M}_3(f)$ we have
\begin{align*}
    \widetilde{M}_3(f) \leq  (c+cs^{-\frac{\tau}{d}})^4 + (c +s^{-\frac{\tau}{d}})^2(c + \log s^{\frac{1}{d}}) + s(c + \log s^{\frac{1}{d}})^2 +s(c +s^{\frac{2\tau}{d}}).
\end{align*}

Thus, using these bounds in Corollary~\ref{normalapp1k2} we arrive at the following statement.

\begin{theorem} \label{CLT:sumsedgelengthscritical}
    Let $\tau > 0$. Assume $\bar{\varphi}$ satisfies Assumption \ref{edgelength-ass1} with (positive) exponent $\alpha = 2\tau + d$. Then there is a constant $C \in (0, \infty)$ independent of $s$ such that
    \begin{align*}
	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{Wass}}\left(\frac{L_s(\tau)-\E L_s(\tau)}{\sqrt{\Var L_s(\tau)}},N\right) & \le C \frac{s^{\frac{\tau}{d}-\frac12}}{\log s^{\frac{1}{d}}}\\
	\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{K}}\left(\frac{L_s(\tau)-\E L_s(\tau)}{\sqrt{\Var L_s(\tau)}},N\right) & \le  C \frac{s^{\frac{\tau}{d}-\frac12}}{\log s^{\frac{1}{d}}}.
\end{align*}
In particular, if $\tau \in (0, \frac{d}{2}]$, a central limit theorem holds.
\end{theorem}


Again note that Theorem \ref{CLT:sumsedgelengthscritical} only yieldls a central limit theorem for $\tau\in (0, \frac{d}{2}]$. Therefore, once more we use a truncation technique to obtain a central limit theorem for $\tau > \frac{d}{2}$ as well. For $\tau>0$ define the truncated statistic
\[
\overline{L}_s(\tau)
:= \frac{1}{2}\sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in \eta^2_{\neq}\cap \Gamma_s^2}
\mathbf{1}\!\left\{x_1\leftrightarrow x_2,\ \|x_1-x_2\|\le s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}}\right\}
\|x_1-x_2\|^{\tau}.
\]
By Mecke’s formula and the same change-of-variables as above,
\[
\mathbb{E}\!\left[L_s(\tau)-\overline{L}_s(\tau)\right]
= \frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma_s}\!\!\int_{\Gamma_s}
\mathbf{1}_{\{\|x-y\|>s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}}\}}\,
\varphi(x-y)\,\|x-y\|^{\tau}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y
\;\sim\;
s\!\int_{s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}}}^{\infty}\! r^{\tau}\,\bar{\varphi}(r)\,r^{d-1}\,\mathrm{d}r.
\]
In the critical case $\alpha=2\tau+d$ we have $\bar{\varphi}(r)\sim cr^{-\alpha}$, hence
\[
\int_{s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}}}^{\infty} r^{\tau}\,r^{-\alpha}\,r^{d-1}\,\mathrm{d}r
=\int_{s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}}}^{\infty} r^{-(\tau+1)}\,\mathrm{d}r
\;\asymp\; \big(s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}}\big)^{-\tau}
= s^{-\frac{1}{2}}.
\]
Therefore
\begin{equation}\label{eq:L1gap_critical}
\mathbb{E}\!\left[L_s(\tau)-\overline{L}_s(\tau)\right]
\;\asymp\; \sqrt{s}.
\end{equation}
Since in the critical regime $\Var L_s(\tau)\asymp s\log s$ (see Lemma~\ref{lem:varlength} and the discussion above),
the standardized $L^1$ gap satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:std_gap_critical}
\frac{\mathbb{E}\!\left[L_s(\tau)-\overline{L}_s(\tau)\right]}
{\sqrt{\Var L_s(\tau)}}
\;\asymp\; \frac{\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{s\log s}}
\;=\; \frac{1}{\sqrt{\log s}}.
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:critical_truncation_gap}
Let $\tau>0$ and $\alpha=2\tau+d$. Then
\[
\frac{\mathbb{E}\!\left|L_s(\tau)-\overline{L}_s(\tau)\right|}
{\sqrt{\Var[L_s(\tau)]}}
\;\le\; \frac{C}{\sqrt{\log s}},
\]
for some $C\in(0,\infty)$ independent of $s$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By \eqref{eq:L1gap_critical} we have $\E|L_s-\overline{L}_s|\asymp\sqrt{s}$.
If we divide by $\sqrt{\Var[L_s(\tau)]}\asymp \sqrt{s\log s}$ we obtain \eqref{eq:std_gap_critical}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:critical_truncated_CLT}
In the setting $\tau>0$, $\alpha=2\tau+d$, the truncated statistic $\overline{L}_s(\tau)$
satisfies a central limit theorem with
\[
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{Wass}}\!\left(
\frac{\overline{L}_s(\tau)-\E[\overline{L}_s(\tau)]}{\sqrt{\Var [\overline{L}_s(\tau)]}},\,N\right)
\;\le\; C\,s^{-1/2},
\qquad
\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{K}}\!\left(
\frac{\overline{L}_s(\tau)-\E[\overline{L}_s(\tau)]}{\sqrt{\Var[\overline{L}_s(\tau)]}},\,N\right)
\;\le\; C\,s^{-1/2}.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We repeat the moment bounds as in Lemma~\ref{lem:CLT_truncated}, now with the cutoff
$a=s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}}$. The inner integrals are restricted to $\|u\|\le a$, and their growth
in $a$ induces at most polylogarithmic factors at the critical exponent;
the resulting $M_{ij}$ terms are still $O(s\,\mathrm{polylog}(s))$.
Moreover, $\Var\overline{L}_s(\tau)\asymp s\log s$, so Corollary~\ref{normalapp1k2}
yields the stated $s^{-1/2}$ rate.
\end{proof}




Combining Lemma~\ref{lem:critical_truncation_gap} with Lemma~\ref{lem:critical_truncated_CLT}
and using that $\Var[L_s(\tau)] \asymp s\log s$, we obtain that the difference between
$L_s(\tau)$ and its truncated version is negligible at the scale $\sqrt{\Var[L_s(\tau)]}$.
Together with the central limit theorem for $\overline{L}_s(\tau)$, this yields the following.

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:CLTedgelengths_critical_full}
Let $\tau>0$ and assume $\bar{\varphi}$ satisfies Assumption \ref{edgelength-ass1} with exponent
$\alpha=2\tau+d$. Then, as $s\to\infty$,
\[
\frac{L_s(\tau)-\E[L_s(\tau)]}{\sqrt{\Var[L_s(\tau)]}}
\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}
N.
\]
In particular, a central limit theorem holds for $L_s(\tau)$ in the critical regime.
\end{theorem}






%$$
%\overline{L}_s(\tau) = \frac{1}{2} \sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta_{s,\neq}^2\cap\Gamma_s^2} \mathbf{1}\{ x_1\leftrightarrow x_2, \|x_1-x_2\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}} \} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau
%$$

%$$
%\mathbb{E}[L_s(\tau) - \overline{L}_s(\tau)] \sim s \int_{s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}}}^\infty r^{\tau} r^{-\alpha} r^{d-1} \, \, \mathrm{d}r \sim s (s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}})^{\tau-\alpha+d} =  s (s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}})^{-\tau}= \sqrt{s}
%$$

%$$
%\frac{\mathbb{E}[L_s(\tau) - \overline{L}_s(\tau)]}{\sqrt{\operatorname{Var} L_s(\tau)}} \sim \frac{\sqrt{s}}{\sqrt{s \log s}} = \frac{1}{\sqrt{\log s}}
%$$

%$$
%s \int_0^{s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}}} r^{4\tau} r^{-\alpha} r^{d-1} \, \mathrm{d} r \sim s (s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}})^{4\tau-\alpha+d} = s (s^{\frac{1}{2\tau}})^{2\tau} =s^2
%$$







\subsubsection{Regimes with stable and non-Gaussian limit theorems}\label{nongaussianregime}

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:stable}
Let $\alpha\in[2d,\infty)$. Define the measure $\mathbf{L}$ on $(0,\infty)$ by $\mathbf{L}([u,\infty))=c_{\bar\varphi}u^{d-\alpha}$ for $u\in(0,\infty)$ if $\alpha>2d$ and
$$
\mathbf{L}([u,\infty))= \frac{\gamma^2l_{\bar{\varphi}}}{2} \int_{\R^d}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{\|v\|\geq u\Big\}\, 
\lambda_d\big(\Gamma\cap(\Gamma-v)\big) \|v\|^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}v
$$
for $u\in(0,\infty)$ if $\alpha=2d$ and let $\zeta$ be Poisson process on $(0,\infty)$ with intensity measure $\mathbf{L}$.
\begin{itemize}
\item [a)] For $\tau\in (\alpha-d,\infty)$,
$$
s^{\tau/(d-\alpha)} L_s(\tau) \overset{d}{\longrightarrow} \sum_{t\in \zeta} t^\tau \quad \text{as} \quad s\to\infty.
$$
\item [b)] For $\tau=\alpha-d$,
$$
s^{\tau/(d-\alpha)} \bigg(L_s(\tau) - \frac{1}{2} \mathbb{E}\sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}} \mathbf{1}\{\|x_1-x_2\|\leq 1\} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau \bigg) \overset{d}{\longrightarrow} S \quad \text{as} \quad s\to\infty
$$
with a random variable $S$ given by
$$
S=\lim_{a\to0} \left(\sum_{t\in\zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in\zeta\cap(a,1]} t^\tau\right). 
$$
\item [c)] For $\tau\in (\frac{\alpha-d}{2},\alpha-d)$,
$$
s^{\tau/(d-\alpha)} (L_s(\tau) - \mathbb{E}[L_s(\tau))] \overset{d}{\longrightarrow} T \quad \text{as} \quad s\to\infty
$$
with a random variable $T$ given by
$$
T=\lim_{a\to0} \left(\sum_{t\in\zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in\zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau\right). 
$$
\end{itemize}
\end{theorem}



First let us discuss the existence of the limiting distributions in Theorem \ref{thm:stable}. The measure $\mathbf{L}$ has the density $(0,\infty)\ni t\mapsto c_{\bar{\varphi}} (\alpha-d) t^{d-\alpha-1}$ for $\alpha>2d$, while it is dominated by a measure with this density for $\alpha=2d$, which we will use in the sequel.

For $\tau>\frac{\alpha-d}{2}$ one has almost surely $\zeta([1,\infty))<\infty$ and, thus, $\sum_{t\in\zeta\cap[1,\infty)}t^\tau<\infty$. In case that $\tau>\alpha-d$ the Mecke formula yields
$$
\mathbb{E}\sum_{t\in\zeta\cap(0,1)} t^\tau = \int_0^1 t^\tau \, \mathbf{L}(\mathrm{d}t) \leq \int_0^1 t^\tau c_{\bar{\varphi}} (\alpha-d) t^{d-\alpha-1} \, \mathrm{d}t = \frac{c_{\bar{\varphi}} (\alpha-d)}{\tau+d-\alpha}
$$
so that $\sum_{t\in\zeta\cap(0,1)}t^\tau<\infty$ almost surely. For $\tau\in(\frac{\alpha-d}{2},\alpha-d)$ one has
$$
\sum_{k=1}^\infty \operatorname{Var}\bigg[ \sum_{t\in\zeta\cap[1/(k+1),1/k)} t^\tau \bigg] = \sum_{k=1}^\infty \int_{1/(k+1)}^{1/k} t^{2\tau} \, \mathbf{L}(\mathrm{d}t) \leq \int_0^1 t^{2\tau} c_{\bar{\varphi}} (\alpha-d) t^{d-\alpha-1} \, \mathrm{d}t  = \frac{c_{\bar{\varphi}}(\alpha-d)}{2\tau+d-\alpha}, 
$$
where the first identity is a consequence of a short computation using the Mecke formula. Thus, by \cite[Lemma 5.16]{Kallenberg_2023},
$$
\sum_{k=1}^\infty \bigg( \sum_{t\in\zeta\cap[1/(k+1),1/k)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in\zeta\cap[1/(k+1),1/k)} t^\tau \bigg) = \lim_{n\to\infty} \sum_{t\in\zeta\cap(1/n,1)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E}\sum_{t\in\zeta\cap(1/n,1)} t^\tau  
$$
converges almost surely. Combining these arguments provides that the almost sure limits in the definitions of the limiting random variables in Theorem \ref{thm:stable} are all well defined.

Next we show that the limiting distributions in Theorem \ref{thm:stable} are alpha-stable distributions for $\alpha\in(2d,\infty)$. Let $m\in\mathbb{N}$ and let $\zeta^{(1)},\hdots,\zeta^{(m)}$ be independent copies of $\zeta$. Because of
$$
m\mathbf{L}([u,\infty)) = \mathbf{L}([m^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}u,\infty)) 
$$
for $u\in(0,\infty)$, we have
$$
\zeta^{(1)}+\hdots+\zeta^{(m)}\overset{d}{=}m^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\zeta.
$$
For $\tau\in(\alpha-d,\infty)$ this implies
$$
\sum_{t\in \zeta^{(1)}} t^\tau + \hdots + \sum_{t\in \zeta^{(m)}} t^\tau = \sum_{t\in \zeta^{(1)}+\hdots+\zeta^{(m)}} t^\tau \overset{d}{=} \sum_{t\in m^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\zeta} t^\tau = m^{\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} \sum_{t\in \zeta} t^\tau,
$$
i.e., $\sum_{t\in \zeta} t^\tau$ follows an alpha-stable distribution with parameter $\frac{\alpha-d}{\tau}$. In case of $\tau=\alpha-d$, one has, for $a\in(0,1)$,
$$
\mathbb{E}\sum_{t\in(a,1)} t^{\tau} = c_{\bar\varphi}\int_a^1 t^{-1} \, \mathrm{d}t = c_{\bar\varphi}\log(a)
$$
and
\begin{align*}
& \sum_{t\in \zeta^{(1)}\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in \zeta^{(1)}\cap(a,1)} t^\tau + \hdots + \sum_{t\in \zeta^{(m)}\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E}\sum_{t\in \zeta^{(m)}\cap(a,1)} t^\tau \\
& = \sum_{t\in (\zeta^{(1)}+\hdots+\zeta^{(m)})\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - m c_{\bar\varphi}\log(a) \\
& \overset{d}{=} \sum_{t\in m^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\zeta\cap (a,1)} t^\tau - m c_{\bar\varphi}\log(a) = m^{\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap (m^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}a,\infty)} t^\tau - m c_{\bar\varphi}\log(a) \\
& = m \bigg(\sum_{t\in \zeta\cap (m^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}a,\infty)} t^\tau - c_{\bar\varphi}\log(m^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}a) \bigg) - \frac{c_{\bar\varphi}}{\alpha-d} m \log(m).
\end{align*}
Letting $a\to0$ proves that
$$
S^{(1)}+\hdots+S^{(m)}\overset{d}{=} m S - \frac{c_{\bar\varphi}}{\alpha-d} m \log(m),
$$
where $S^{(1)},\hdots,S^{(m)}$ are independent copies of $S$. This means that $S$ follows an alpha-stable distribution with parameter $1=\frac{\alpha-d}{\tau}$. Finally, for $\tau\in(\frac{\alpha-d}{2},\alpha-d)$ and $a\in(0,\infty)$, we obtain
$$
\mathbb{E}\sum_{t\in(a,\infty)} t^{\tau} = c_{\bar\varphi}\int_a^\infty t^{\tau+d-\alpha-1} \, \mathrm{d}t = c_{\bar\varphi} a^{\tau+d-\alpha}
$$
and
\begin{align*}
& \sum_{t\in \zeta^{(1)}\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in \zeta^{(1)}\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau + \hdots + \sum_{t\in \zeta^{(m)}\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E}\sum_{t\in \zeta^{(m)}\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau \\
& = \sum_{t\in (\zeta^{(1)}+\hdots+\zeta^{(m)})\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - m c_{\bar\varphi} a^{\tau+d-\alpha}\\
& \overset{d}{=} \sum_{t\in m^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\zeta\cap (a,1)} t^\tau - m c_{\bar\varphi} a^{\tau+d-\alpha} = m^{\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap (m^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}a,\infty)} t^\tau - m c_{\bar\varphi} m^{-\frac{\tau+d-\alpha}{d-\alpha}} (m^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}a)^{\tau+d-\alpha} \\
& = m^{\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} \bigg(\sum_{t\in \zeta\cap (m^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}a,\infty)} t^\tau - c_{\bar\varphi} (m^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}a)^{\tau+d-\alpha} \bigg).
\end{align*}
Now $a\to0$ leads to
$$
T^{(1)}+\hdots+T^{(m)} \overset{d}{=} m^{\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}  T, 
$$
i.e., $T$ is distributed according to an alpha-stable distribution with parameter $\frac{\alpha-d}{\tau}$.

Define
$$
\xi_s:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}\cap \Gamma_s^2} \mathbf{1}\left\{ x_1 \leftrightarrow x_2 \right\}\delta_{s^{1/(d-\alpha)}\|x_1-x_2\|}.
$$

\begin{prop}\label{prop:convergence_point_process_edge_lengths}
Let $\alpha\in[2d,\infty)$, let $\zeta$ be as in Theorem \ref{thm:stable} and let $a\in(0,\infty)$. Then,
\begin{equation}\label{eqn:Bound_KR_lengths}
\mathrm{d}_{\rm KR}(\xi_s|_{[a,\infty)},\zeta|_{[a,\infty)}) \leq R_\alpha + \frac{4\gamma^3 (d\kappa_d C_{\bar{\varphi}})^2}{(\alpha-d)^2 a^{2(\alpha-d)}} \frac{1}{s}
\end{equation}
with
$$
R_\alpha = \frac{\gamma^2}{2}  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \mathbf{1}\Big\{\|v\|\geq a\Big\} \big| (s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)^\alpha
\bar\varphi(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)\,
\lambda_d\big(\Gamma\cap(\Gamma-s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}-\frac{1}{d}}v)\big) - l_{\bar{\varphi}}\big| \, \|v\|^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}v
$$
if $\alpha>2d$ and
$$
R_\alpha = \frac{\gamma^2}{2} \int_{\R^d}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{\|v\|\geq a\Big\}\, \big|(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)^\alpha
\bar\varphi(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|) - l_{\bar{\varphi}}\big|\,
\lambda_d\big(\Gamma\cap(\Gamma-v)\big) \|v\|^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}v
$$
if $\alpha=2d$. Moreover, the right-hand side of \eqref{eqn:Bound_KR_lengths} vanishes as $s\to\infty$ and
$$
\xi_s\overset{d}{\longrightarrow} \zeta \quad \text{as} \quad s\to\infty.
$$
\end{prop}

\begin{proof}
Let $A\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})$ be such that $A\subset[a,\infty)$. The intensity measure of $\xi_s$ is given by
\begin{align*}
\mathbf{L}_s(A)
& = \frac{1}{2}\E \sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}\cap\Gamma_s^2}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{s^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}\|x_1-x_2\|\in A\Big\}\,
\mathbf{1}\{x_1\leftrightarrow x_2\} \\
&= \frac{\gamma^2}{2}\int_{\Gamma_s}\int_{\Gamma_s}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{s^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}\|x_1-x_2\|\in A\Big\}\,
\bar\varphi(\|x_1-x_2\|)\,\mathrm{d}x_1\,\mathrm{d}x_2.
\end{align*}
Equivalently, with the changes of variables $z=y-x$ and $v=s^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}z$,
\begin{align*}
\mathbf{L}_s(A)
& = \frac{\gamma^2}{2}\int_{\R^d}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{s^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}\|z\|\in A\Big\}\,
\bar\varphi(\|z\|)\,
\lambda_d\big(\Gamma_s\cap(\Gamma_s-z)\big)\,\mathrm{d}z \\
& = \frac{\gamma^2}{2} s^{\frac{d}{\alpha-d}} \int_{\R^d}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{\|v\|\in A\Big\}\,
\bar\varphi(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)\,
\lambda_d\big(\Gamma_s\cap(\Gamma_s-s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}v)\big)\,\mathrm{d}v\\
& = \frac{\gamma^2}{2} s^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-d}} \int_{\R^d}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{\|v\|\in A\Big\}\,
\bar\varphi(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)\,
\lambda_d\big(\Gamma\cap(\Gamma-s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}-\frac{1}{d}}v)\big)\,\mathrm{d}v \\
& = \frac{\gamma^2}{2} \int_{\R^d}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{\|v\|\in A\Big\}\, (s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)^\alpha
\bar\varphi(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)\,
\lambda_d\big(\Gamma\cap(\Gamma-s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}-\frac{1}{d}}v)\big) \|v\|^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}v.
\end{align*}
For $\alpha>2d$ we have
$$
\mathbf{L}(A)= c_{\bar\varphi}(\alpha-d)\int_0^\infty \mathbf{1}\{u\in A\}\,u^{d-\alpha-1}\,\mathrm{d}u.
$$
We may substitute $t=u^{\,d-\alpha}$ (equivalently $v=t^{1/(d-\alpha)}$) and use spherical coordinates, yielding
\begin{align*}
\mathbf{L}(A) = \frac{c_{\bar\varphi}(\alpha-d)}{d\kappa_d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \mathbf{1}\{\|v\|\in A\} \|v\|^{-\alpha} \,\mathrm{d}v  = \frac{\gamma^2 l_{\bar{\varphi}}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \mathbf{1}\{\|v\|\in A\} \|v\|^{-\alpha} \,\mathrm{d}v.
\end{align*}
This implies that
\begin{align*}
& \mathrm{d}_{\rm TV}(\mathbf{L}_s|_{[a,\infty)},\mathbf{L}|_{[a,\infty)}) \\
& \leq \frac{\gamma^2}{2}  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \mathbf{1}\Big\{\|v\|\geq a\Big\} \big| (s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)^\alpha
\bar\varphi(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)\,
\lambda_d\big(\Gamma\cap(\Gamma-s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}-\frac{1}{d}}v)\big) - l_{\bar{\varphi}}\big| \, \|v\|^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}v
\end{align*}
if $\alpha>2d$. For $\alpha=2d$ we have
\begin{align*}
& \mathrm{d}_{\rm TV}(\mathbf{L}_s|_{[a,\infty)},\mathbf{L}|_{[a,\infty)}) \\
& \leq \frac{\gamma^2}{2} \int_{\R^d}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{\|v\|\geq a\Big\}\, \big|(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)^\alpha
\bar\varphi(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|) - l_{\bar{\varphi}}\big|\,
\lambda_d\big(\Gamma\cap(\Gamma-v)\big) \|v\|^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}v.
\end{align*}
Because of the convexity of $\Gamma$, one has
\begin{align*}
& \gamma^3 \int_{\Gamma_s^3} \mathbf{1}\{ \|x_1-x_2\|, \|x_1-x_3\| \geq a s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\} \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_2\|) \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_3\|) \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2,x_3) \\
& \leq \gamma^3 \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^3} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{ \frac{x_1+x_2}{2}, \frac{x_1+x_3}{2} \in \Gamma_s \bigg\} \mathbf{1}\{ \|x_1-x_2\|, \|x_1-x_3\| \geq a s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\} \\
& \hspace{2cm} \times \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_2\|) \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_3\|) \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2,x_3).
\end{align*}
The right-hand side is bounded by
$$
\frac{\gamma^3 (d\kappa_d C_{\bar{\varphi}})^2}{(\alpha-d)^2 a^{2(\alpha-d)}} \frac{1}{s}
$$
in the proof of Theorem \ref{thm:edge-length_process}. Exactly as there, one can prove the convergence of the right-hand side of \eqref{eqn:Bound_KR_lengths} to zero and the convergence in distribution of $\xi_s$ to $\zeta$ as $s\to\infty$.
\end{proof}

& = \frac{1}{2}\E \sum_{(z_1,z_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}\cap\Gamma_s^2}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{s^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}\|z_1-z_2\|\in A\Big\}\,
\mathbf{1}\{z_1\leftrightarrow z_2\} \\
&= \frac{\gamma^2}{2}\int_{\Gamma_s}\int_{\Gamma_s}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{s^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}\|x-y\|\in A\Big\}\,
\bar\varphi(\|x-y\|)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y.
\end{align*}

\mathbf{L}_s(A)
& = \frac{\gamma^2}{2}\int_{\R^d}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{s^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}\|z\|\in A\Big\}\,
\bar\varphi(\|z\|)\,
\lambda_d\big(\Gamma_s\cap(\Gamma_s-z)\big)\,\mathrm{d}z \\
& = \frac{\gamma^2}{2} s^{\frac{d}{\alpha-d}} \int_{\R^d}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{\|v\|\in A\Big\}\,
\bar\varphi(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)\,

\mathbf{1}\Big\{\|v\|\in A\Big\}\,
\bar\varphi(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)\,
\lambda_d\big(\Gamma\cap(\Gamma-s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}-\frac{1}{d}}v)\big)\,\mathrm{d}v \\
& = \frac{\gamma^2}{2} \int_{\R^d}
\mathbf{1}\Big\{\|v\|\in A\Big\}\, (s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)^\alpha
\bar\varphi(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}\|v\|)\,
\lambda_d\big(\Gamma\cap(\Gamma-s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}-\frac{1}{d}}v)\big) \|v\|^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}v.
\end{align*}
The intensity measure $\mathbf{L}$ of $\zeta^{(\alpha)}$ is given, for $A\in\mathcal{B}(\R)$ with $A\subset[a,\infty)$, by
\[
\mathbf{L}(A) =  \E[\zeta^{(\alpha)}(A)]
= \E\sum_{t\in\zeta}\mathbf{1}\Big\{t^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}\in A\Big\}
= c_{\bar\varphi}\int_0^\infty \mathbf{1}\Big\{t^{\frac{1}{d-\alpha}}\in A\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t.
\]
We may substitute $t=u^{\,d-\alpha}$ (equivalently $v=t^{1/(d-\alpha)}$) and use spherical coordinates, yielding
\[
\mathbf{L}(A)
= c_{\bar\varphi}(\alpha-d)\int_0^\infty \mathbf{1}\{u\in A\}\,u^{d-\alpha-1}\,\mathrm{d}u = \frac{c_{\bar\varphi}(\alpha-d)}{d\kappa_d}\int_0^\infty \mathbf{1}\{\|v\|\in A\} \|v\|^{-\alpha} \,\mathrm{d}v
%= c_{\bar\varphi}(\alpha-d)\int_A v^{d-\alpha-1}\,\mathrm{d}v.
\]

\end{proof}


\begin{theorem}
Let the assumptions of Theorem \ref{thm:edge-length_process} prevail.
\begin{itemize}
\item [a)] If $(\alpha-d)/\tau\in(0,1)$,
$$
s^{\tau/(d-\alpha)}L_s(\tau) \overset{d}{\longrightarrow} \sum_{t\in\zeta} t^{\tau/(d-\alpha)} \quad \text{as} \quad s\to\infty.
$$
\item [b)] If $(\alpha-d)/\tau=1$,
$$
s^{\tau/(d-\alpha)}(L_s(\tau) - \mathbb{E}[L_s(\tau)])  \overset{d}{\longrightarrow} S \quad \text{as} \quad s\to\infty,
$$
where
$$
S:=\lim_{n\to\infty} \sum_{t\in\zeta\cap[0,n]} t^{-1} - \ln n.
$$
\item [c)] If $(\alpha-d)/\tau\in(1,2)$,
$$
s^{\tau/(d-\alpha)}(L_s(\tau) - \mathbb{E}[L_s(\tau)]) \overset{d}{\longrightarrow} S_\tau \quad \text{as} \quad s\to\infty,
$$
where
$$
S_\tau := \lim_{n\to\infty} \sum_{t\in\zeta\cap[0,n]} t^{\tau/(d-\alpha)} - \mathbb{E}\sum_{t\in\zeta\cap[0,\infty]} t^{\tau/(d-\alpha)}.
$$
\end{itemize}
\end{theorem}



For $\tau\in(0,\infty)$ we consider the edge-length functionals
$$
\widetilde{L}_s^{(\tau)} = \frac{1}{2} \sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{ x_1\leftrightarrow x_2, \frac{x_1+x_2}{2}\in\Gamma_s \bigg\} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau
$$
for $s\geq 1$.

For $a\in(0,\infty)$ and $s\geq 1$ we define
$$
L_{s,>a}(\tau) = \frac{1}{2} \sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}\cap\Gamma_s^2} \mathbf{1}\big\{x_1\leftrightarrow x_2,\|x_1-x_2\|>s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a\big\} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau
$$
and
$$
L_{s,\leq a}(\tau) = \frac{1}{2} \sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}\cap\Gamma_s^2} \mathbf{1}\{x_1\leftrightarrow x_2, \|x_1-x_2\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a\} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau.
$$
Note that $L_s(\tau)=L_{s,>a}(\tau)+L_{s,\leq a}(\tau)$.

\mathbb{P}( s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} \widetilde{L}_{s,>a}^{(\tau)} \leq u ) = \mathbb{P}\bigg( \sum_{t\in\tilde{\xi}_s\cap (a,\infty)} t^{\tau} \leq u\bigg) 
$$
and
$$
\mathbb{P}\bigg( \sum_{t\in\zeta\cap[0,a^{d-\alpha})} t^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} \leq u \bigg) = \mathbb{P}\bigg( \sum_{t\in\zeta^{(\alpha)}\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau \leq u \bigg).
$$
This leads to

\bigg| \mathbb{P}( s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} \widetilde{L}_{s,>a}^{(\tau)} \leq u ) - \mathbb{P}\bigg( \sum_{t\in\zeta\cap[0,a^{d-\alpha})} t^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} \leq u \bigg) \bigg| \leq {\rm d}_{\rm KR}(\tilde{\xi}_s,\zeta^{(\alpha)})
$$

$$
