\begin{lemma}\label{lem:truncation_edge_length}
Let $\alpha\in[2d,\infty)$ and $a>0$.
\begin{itemize}
\item [a)] For $\tau\in(\alpha-d,\infty)$,
$$
\limsup_{s\to \infty} s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} \mathbb{E}[L_{s,\leq a}(\tau)] \leq \frac{C_{\bar{\varphi}}\gamma^2 }{2(\tau-\alpha+d)} a^{\tau-\alpha+d}.
$$
\item [b)] For $\tau\in(\frac{\alpha-d}{2},\alpha-d]$,
$$\limsup_{s\to\infty} s^{-\frac{2\tau}{\alpha-d}} \operatorname{Var}[L_{s,\leq a}(\tau)] \leq \frac{C_{\bar{\varphi}}\gamma^2 }{2(2\tau-\alpha+d)} a^{2\tau-\alpha+d}.$$
\end{itemize}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For $\tau>\alpha-d$ and $s\geq 1$ we have
\begin{align*}
s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}\mathbb{E} L_{s,\leq a}(\tau) & = \frac{s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}}{2} \mathbb{E} \sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}\cap \Gamma_s^2} \mathbf{1}\big\{\|x_1-x_2\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a\big\} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau \\
& = \frac{\gamma^2 s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}}{2} \int_{\Gamma_s^2} \mathbf{1}\big\{\|x_1-x_2\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a\big\} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_2\|) \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2) \\
& \le \frac{\gamma^2 s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}}{2} \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^2} \mathbf{1}\{y\in\Gamma_s,\|z\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a\} \|z\|^\tau \bar{\varphi}(\|z\|) \, \mathrm{d}(y,z) \\
& \le \frac{C_{\bar{\varphi}}\gamma^2 s^{1-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}}{2} \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^2} \mathbf{1}\{\|z\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a\} \|z\|^{\tau-\alpha} \, \mathrm{d}(y,z),
\end{align*}
where we used \eqref{eqn:Upper_bound_bar_varphi} in the last step. Spherical coordinates lead to
\begin{align*}
s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}\mathbb{E}[L_{s,\leq a}(\tau)] & \leq \frac{C_{\bar{\varphi}}\gamma^2 s^{1-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}}{2} \int_0^{s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a} r^{\tau-\alpha+d-1} \, \mathrm{d}r = \frac{C_{\bar{\varphi}}\gamma^2 }{2(\tau-\alpha+d)}s^{1-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} s^{\frac{\tau-\alpha+d}{\alpha-d}} a^{\tau-\alpha+d} \\
& = \frac{C_{\bar{\varphi}}\gamma^2 }{2(\tau-\alpha+d)} a^{\tau-\alpha+d},
\end{align*}
which proves part a).

Next assume that $\tau\in(\frac{\alpha-d}{2},\alpha-d]$. Then, one has
\begin{align*}
\operatorname{Var}[L_{s,\leq a}(\tau)] & \leq \frac{\gamma^2}{2} \int_{\Gamma_s^2} \mathbf{1}\big\{\|x_1-x_2\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a\big\} \|x_1-x_2\|^{2\tau} \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_2\|) \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2) \\
& \quad + \gamma^3 \int_{\Gamma_s^3} \mathbf{1}\big\{\|x_1-x_2\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a, \|x_1-x_2\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a\big\} \\
& \hspace{2cm} \quad \times \|x_1-x_2\|^{\tau} \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_2\|) \|x_1-x_3\|^{\tau} \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_3\|)  \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2,x_3) \\
& =: I_1 + I_2
\end{align*}
for $s\geq 1$. Arguing as above yields
$$
s^{-\frac{2\tau}{\alpha-d}}I_1 \leq \frac{C_{\bar{\varphi}}\gamma^2 }{2(2\tau-\alpha+d)} a^{2\tau-\alpha+d}.
$$
Substitution leads to
\begin{align*}
s^{-\frac{2\tau}{\alpha-d}}I_2 & \leq s^{-\frac{2\tau}{\alpha-d}} \gamma^3 \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^3} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{x_1\in\Gamma_s,\|x_1-x_2\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a, \|x_1-x_2\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a\bigg\} \\
& \hspace{2cm} \quad \times \|x_1-x_2\|^{\tau} \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_2\|) \|x_1-x_3\|^{\tau} \bar{\varphi}(\|x_1-x_3\|)  \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2,x_3) \\
& = s^{-\frac{2\tau}{\alpha-d}} \gamma^3 \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^3} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{y\in\Gamma_s,\|z_1\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a, \|z_2\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a\bigg\} \\
& \hspace{2cm} \quad \times \|z_1\|^{\tau} \bar{\varphi}(\|z_1\|) \|z_2\|^{\tau} \bar{\varphi}(\|z_2\|)  \, \mathrm{d}(y,z_1,z_2) \\
& = s^{1-\frac{2\tau}{\alpha-d}} \gamma^3 \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^2} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{\|z_1\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a, \|z_2\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a\bigg\} \|z_1\|^{\tau} \bar{\varphi}(\|z_1\|) \|z_2\|^{\tau} \bar{\varphi}(\|z_2\|)  \, \mathrm{d}(z_1,z_2). %\\
%& \leq C_{\bar{\varphi}} s^{1-\frac{2\tau}{\alpha-d}} \gamma^3 \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^2} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{\|z_1\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a, \|z_2\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a\bigg\}  \|z_1\|^{\tau-\alpha} \|z_2\|^{\tau-\alpha} \, \mathrm{d}(z_1,z_2).
\end{align*}
By spherical coordinates and \eqref{eqn:Upper_bound_bar_varphi} we obtain
\begin{align*}
\int_{\mathbb{R}^d}  \mathbf{1}\Big\{\|z\|\leq s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a\Big\} \|z\|^{\tau} \bar{\varphi}(\|z\|) \, \mathrm{d}z & = d\kappa_d \int_0^{s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a} r^{\tau+d-1} \bar{\varphi}(r) \, \mathrm{d}r \\
& \leq d\kappa_d + d\kappa_d \int_1^{s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a} r^{\tau+d-1} \bar{\varphi}(r) \, \mathrm{d}r \\
& \leq d\kappa_d + d\kappa_d C_{\bar{\varphi}} \int_1^{s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a} r^{\tau+d-1-\alpha} \, \mathrm{d}r.
\end{align*}
We have
$$
\int_1^{s^{\frac{1}{\alpha-d}}a} r^{\tau+d-1-\alpha} \, \mathrm{d}r = \begin{cases} \frac{1-s^{\frac{\tau+d-\alpha}{\alpha-d}}a^{\tau+d-\alpha}}{\tau+d-\alpha}, & \quad \tau \in (\frac{\alpha-d}{2},\alpha-d), \\ \frac{\log s}{\alpha-d}+\log(a), & \quad \tau=\alpha-d,\end{cases}
$$
where the exponent of $s$ is negative in the first case. Thus, we see that
$$
\lim_{s\to\infty} s^{-\frac{2\tau}{\alpha-d}}I_2 = 0.
$$
This implies
$$
\limsup_{s\to\infty} s^{-\frac{2\tau}{\alpha-d}} \operatorname{Var}[L_{s,\leq a}(\tau)] \leq \frac{C_{\bar{\varphi}}\gamma^2 }{2(2\tau-\alpha+d)} a^{2\tau-\alpha+d},
$$
which proves part b).
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem \ref{thm:stable}]
Let $a\in(0,\infty)$ be fixed. Note that, for $u\in\mathbb{R}$ and $s\geq 1$,
$$
\mathbb{P}( s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} L_{s,>a}(\tau) \leq u ) = \mathbb{P}\bigg( \sum_{t\in\xi_s\cap (a,\infty)} t^{\tau} \leq u\bigg).
$$
This leads to
$$
\bigg| \mathbb{P}( s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} L_{s,>a}(\tau) \leq u ) - \mathbb{P}\bigg( \sum_{t\in\zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^{\tau} \leq u \bigg) \bigg| \leq {\rm d}_{\rm KR}(\xi_s,\zeta)
$$
so that, by Proposition \ref{prop:convergence_point_process_edge_lengths},
\begin{equation}\label{eqn:convergence_truncated}
s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} L_{s,>a}(\tau) \overset{d}{\longrightarrow} \sum_{t\in\zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^{\tau} \quad \text{as} \quad s\to\infty.
\end{equation}

Let $\operatorname{Lip}_{1,1}$ be the set of all functions $h:\mathbb{R}\to[-1,1]$ with Lipschitz constant one and recall that these functions metrise convergence in distribution of random variables.

First assume that $\tau>\alpha-d$. For $h\in\operatorname{Lip}_{1,1}$, $a\in(0,\infty)$ and $s\geq 1$, one has
\begin{align*}
& \bigg| \mathbb{E}h(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}L_s(\tau)) - \mathbb{E}h\bigg(\sum_{t\in\zeta} t^\tau\bigg) \bigg| \\
& \leq \big| \mathbb{E}h(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}L_s(\tau)) - \mathbb{E}h(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}L_{s,>a}(\tau)) \big| + \bigg| \mathbb{E}h(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}L_{s,>a}(\tau)) - \mathbb{E}h\bigg(\sum_{t\in\zeta\cap (a,\infty)} t^\tau\bigg) \bigg| \\
& \quad + \bigg|\mathbb{E}h\bigg(\sum_{t\in\zeta\cap (a,\infty)} t^\tau\bigg) -  \mathbb{E}h\bigg(\sum_{t\in\zeta} t^\tau\bigg) \bigg|.
\end{align*}
From \eqref{eqn:convergence_truncated} it follows that
\begin{align*}
& \limsup_{s\to\infty} \bigg| \mathbb{E}h(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}L_s(\tau)) - \mathbb{E}h\bigg(\sum_{t\in\zeta} t^\tau\bigg) \bigg| \\
& \leq \limsup_{s\to\infty} \big| \mathbb{E}h(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}L_s(\tau)) - \mathbb{E}h(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}L_{s,> a}(\tau)) \big| + \bigg|\mathbb{E}h\bigg(\sum_{t\in\zeta\cap (a,\infty)} t^\tau\bigg) -  \mathbb{E}h\bigg(\sum_{t\in\zeta} t^\tau\bigg) \bigg|.
\end{align*}
Here, the second term vanishes as $a\to0$. On the other hand, by Lemma \ref{lem:truncation_edge_length} a),
$$
\limsup_{s\to\infty} \bigg| \mathbb{E}h(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}L_s(\tau)) - \mathbb{E}h\bigg(\sum_{t\in\zeta} t^\tau\bigg) \bigg| \leq \limsup_{s\to\infty} s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} \mathbb{E}L_{s,\leq a}(\tau)\leq \frac{C_{\bar{\varphi}}\gamma^2 }{2(\tau-\alpha+d)} a^{\tau-\alpha+d}, 
$$
where the right-hand side converges to zero as $a\to\infty$. Thus, we have shown that
$$
\lim_{s\to\infty} \mathbb{E}h(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}L_s(\tau)) = \mathbb{E}h\bigg(\sum_{t\in\zeta} t^\tau\bigg),
$$
which completes the proof of part a).

Next we assume $\tau\in(\frac{\alpha-d}{2},\alpha-d)$. For $h\in\operatorname{Lip}_{1,1}$, $a\in(0,\infty)$ and $s\geq 1$ we obtain
\begin{align*}
& \big| \mathbb{E} h\big(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}(L_s(\tau)-\mathbb{E}L_s(\tau))\big)  - \mathbb{E} h(T)\big| \\
& \leq \big| \mathbb{E} h\big(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}(L_s(\tau)-\mathbb{E}[L_s(\tau)])\big) - \mathbb{E} h\big(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}(L_{s,>a}(\tau)-\mathbb{E}L_{s,>a}(\tau))\big) \big| \\
& \quad + \bigg| \mathbb{E} h\big(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}(L_{s,>a}(\tau)-\mathbb{E}[L_{s,>a}(\tau)])\big) - \mathbb{E} h\bigg( \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau \bigg) \bigg| \\
& \quad + \bigg| \mathbb{E} h\bigg( \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau \bigg)  - \mathbb{E} h(T)\bigg| \\
& =: R_1(a,s) + R_2(a,s) + R_3(a).
\end{align*}
From \textcolor{cyan}{???} we deduce
$$
\lim_{s\to\infty} s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} \mathbb{E}L_{s,>a}(\tau) = \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau
$$
so that, together with \eqref{prop:convergence_point_process_edge_lengths},
$$
s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}(L_{s,>a}(\tau)-\mathbb{E}L_{s,>a}(\tau)) \overset{d}{\longrightarrow} \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau \quad \text{as} \quad s\to\infty.
$$
This yields
$$
\lim_{s\to\infty} R_2(a,s) =0.
$$
From Lemma \ref{lem:truncation_edge_length} b) it follows that
\begin{align*}
\limsup_{s\to\infty} R_1(a,s) & \leq \limsup_{s\to\infty} s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} \mathbb{E} \big| L_{s,>a}(\tau) - \mathbb{E} L_{s,>a}(\tau) \big| \leq \limsup_{s\to\infty} s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}} \sqrt{\operatorname{Var}[L_{s,>a}(\tau) }]   \\
& \leq \frac{\sqrt{C_{\bar{\varphi}}}\gamma }{\sqrt{2(2\tau-\alpha+d)}} a^{\tau-\frac{\alpha+d}{2}},
\end{align*}
where the right-hand side vanishes as $a\to0$. By the definition of $T$ one has $R_3(a)\to0$ as $a\to 0$. Thus, first letting $s\to\infty$ and then $a\to 0$ leads to
$$
\lim_{s\to\infty} \mathbb{E} h(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}(L_s(\tau)-\mathbb{E}L_s(\tau)))  = \mathbb{E} h(T),
$$
which is part c).

Finally, we argue analogously for $\tau=\alpha-d$. For $h\in\operatorname{Lip}_{1,1}$, $s\geq 1$ and $a\in(0,\infty)$ one has
\begin{align*}
& \bigg| \mathbb{E} h\bigg(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}\bigg(L_s(\tau)-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}} \mathbf{1}\{\|x_1-x_2\|\leq 1\} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau\bigg)\bigg)  - \mathbb{E} h(S)\bigg| \\
& \leq \bigg| \mathbb{E} h\bigg(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}\bigg(L_s(\tau)-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}} \mathbf{1}\{\|x_1-x_2\|\leq 1\} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau\bigg)\bigg) \\
& \quad \quad - \mathbb{E} h\bigg(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}\bigg(L_{s,>a}(\tau)-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}} \mathbf{1}\{a<\|x_1-x_2\|\leq 1\} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau\bigg)\bigg) \bigg| \\
& \quad + \bigg| \mathbb{E} h\bigg(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}\bigg(L_{s,>a}(\tau)-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}} \mathbf{1}\{a<\|x_1-x_2\|\leq 1\} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau\bigg)\bigg) \\
& \quad \quad - \mathbb{E} h\bigg( \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau \bigg)\bigg| \\
& \quad + \bigg| \mathbb{E} h\bigg( \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau \bigg) -\mathbb{E}h(S) \bigg| \\
& =: R_1(s,a) + R_2(a,s) + R_3(a).
\end{align*}
From Lemma \ref{lem:truncation_edge_length} we derive that
$$
\lim_{a\to 0} \limsup_{s\to\infty} R_1(a,s) = 0,
$$
while the definition of $S$ yields $\lim_{a\to0} R_3(a)=0$. Because of
$$
s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}\mathbb{E}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}} \mathbf{1}\{\|x_1-x_2\|\leq 1\} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau = \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(0,\infty)} t^\tau,
$$
which follows from \textcolor{cyan}{???}, and \eqref{eqn:convergence_truncated}, we see that
$$
s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}\bigg(L_{s,>a}(\tau)-\mathbb{E}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}} \mathbf{1}\{a<\|x_1-x_2\|\leq 1\} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau\bigg)\bigg) \overset{d}{\longrightarrow} \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau - \mathbb{E} \sum_{t\in \zeta\cap(a,\infty)} t^\tau
$$
as $s\to\infty$. This shows that $\lim_{s\to\infty} R_2(a,s)=0$. Combining the above estimates yields
$$
\lim_{s\to\infty} \mathbb{E} h\bigg(s^{-\frac{\tau}{\alpha-d}}\bigg(L_s(\tau)-\mathbb{E}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}} \mathbf{1}\{\|x_1-x_2\|\leq 1\} \|x_1-x_2\|^\tau\bigg)\bigg)  = \mathbb{E} h(S),
$$
which gives c).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Proofs for Hub-to-hub edges}


\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:hub-to-hub-edges}]
We start with the proof of part a). In order to apply Corollary \ref{cor:Poisson_approximation}, we need to compute
\begin{align*}
r_0:= & \gamma^3 \mathbb{E} \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^3} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{ \frac{x_1+x_2}{2}, \frac{x_1+x_3}{2}\in \Gamma_s \bigg\} \mathbf{1}\{W_1,W_2,W_3\geq t_s\} \varphi(\kappa(W_1,W_2)/\|x_1-x_2\|^d) \\
& \hspace{3cm} \times \varphi(\kappa(W_1,W_3)/\|x_1-x_3\|^d) \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2,x_3).
\end{align*}
Using substitution, this can be bounded by
\begin{align*}
r_0 & = \gamma^3 \mathbb{E} \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^3} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{ \frac{x_1+x_2}{2}, \frac{2x_1+y}{2}\in \Gamma_s \bigg\} \mathbf{1}\{W_1,W_2,W_3\geq t_s\} \varphi(\kappa(W_1,W_2)/\|x_1-x_2\|^d) \\
& \hspace{3cm} \times \varphi(\kappa(W_1,W_3)/\|y\|^d) \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2,y) \\
& \leq \gamma^3 \mathbb{E} \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^3} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{ \frac{x_1+x_2}{2}\in \Gamma_s \bigg\} \mathbf{1}\{W_1,W_2,W_3\geq t_s\}\kappa(W_1,W_3) \varphi(\kappa(W_1,W_2)/\|x_1-x_2\|^d) \\
& \hspace{3cm} \times \varphi(1/\|y'\|^d) \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2,y') \\
& = c_\varphi \gamma^3 \mathbb{E} \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^2} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{ \frac{x_1+x_2}{2}\in \Gamma_s \bigg\} \mathbf{1}\{W_1,W_2,W_3\geq t_s\}\kappa(W_1,W_3) \varphi(\kappa(W_1,W_2)/\|x_1-x_2\|^d) \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2).
\end{align*}
By the same arguments as for the computation of $\mathbb{E}[E_s]$ above, we obtain
$$
r_0 \leq \gamma^3 c_\varphi^2 s \mathbb{E}[ \mathbf{1}\{ W_1,W_2,W_3\geq t_s \} \kappa(W_1,W_2) \kappa(W_1,W_3)]
$$
Now an application of Corollary \ref{cor:Poisson_approximation} proves part a).

For part b) we evaluate the terms arising in Corollary \ref{normalapp1k2}. By substitution we have that
\begin{align*}
\widetilde{M}_1(f) & =16\gamma^5 \mathbb{E}\int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^5} \prod_{j=2}^5 \mathbf{1}\bigg\{ \frac{x_1+x_j}{2}\in\Gamma_s \bigg\} \varphi(\kappa(W_1,W_j)/\|x_1-x_j\|^d) \\
& \hspace{3cm} \times \mathbf{1}\{W_1,\hdots,W_5\geq t_s\} \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,\hdots,x_5) \\
& =16\gamma^5 \lambda_d(\Gamma_s) c_\varphi^4 \mathbb{E} \prod_{j=2}^5 \kappa(W_1,W_j) \mathbf{1}\{W_1,\hdots,W_5\geq t_s\}.
\end{align*}
Similar calculations show that
\begin{align*}
\widetilde{M}_2(f) & = 16 \gamma^4 s c_\varphi^3 \mathbb{E}[ \kappa(W_1,W_2) \kappa(W_1,W_3) \kappa(W_1,W_4) \mathbf{1}\{W_1,\hdots,W_4\geq t_s\} ] \\
& \quad + 16 \gamma^4 s c_\varphi^3 \mathbb{E}[ \kappa(W_1,W_2) \kappa(W_3,W_4) \kappa(W_1,W_3) \mathbf{1}\{W_1,\hdots,W_4\geq t_s\}] \\
& \quad + 32 \gamma^5 s c_\varphi^4 \mathbb{E}[\kappa(W_1,W_2) \kappa(W_3,W_4) \kappa(W_1,W_5)\kappa(W_3,W_5) \mathbf{1}\{W_1,\hdots,W_5\geq t_s\}] 
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
\widetilde{M}_3(f) & = 144 \gamma^3 s c_\varphi^2  \mathbb{E}[\kappa(W_1,W_2)\kappa(W_1,W_3) \mathbf{1}\{W_1,\hdots,W_3\geq t_s\}] \\
& \quad + 8\gamma s c_\varphi \mathbb{E}[\kappa(W_1,W_2) \mathbf{1}\{W_1,W_2\geq t_s\}].
\end{align*}
This means that $\widetilde{M}_1(f)$, $\widetilde{M}_2(f)$ and $\widetilde{M}_3(f)$ are bounded by constant multiples of $R_s$. For the variance we have the lower bound
\begin{align*}
\operatorname{Var}[E_s] & \geq \frac{\gamma^2}{4} \mathbb{E} \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^2} \mathbf{1}\{z\in \Gamma_s\} \mathbf{1}\{W_1,W_2\geq t_s\}  \varphi(\kappa(W_1,W_2)/\|u\|^d) \, \mathrm{d}(z,u) \\
& = \frac{\gamma^2 s}{4} c_\varphi \mathbb{E}[\kappa(W_1,W_2) \mathbf{1}\{W_1,W_2\geq t_s\}],
\end{align*}
where we used the computation for the expectation above. Thus, the statement of part b) follows from Corollary \ref{normalapp1k2}.
\end{proof}


\begin{proof}[Proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:hub-to-hub-edges1}]
One has to evaluate the bounds of Theorem \ref{thm:hub-to-hub-edges} for the particular form of $\kappa$. For part b) we note that
\begin{align*}
R_s & = \sqrt{\mathbb{E}[W^3 \mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}]} \mathbb{E}[W \mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}]^{3/2} + \sqrt{\mathbb{E}[W^4 \mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}]} \mathbb{E}[W \mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}]^2 \\
& \quad + \big( 1 + \sqrt{\mathbb{E}[W^2 \mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}]} + \mathbb{E}[W^2 \mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}] + \sqrt{\mathbb{E}[W^2 \mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}]}^3\big) \mathbb{E}[W \mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}],
\end{align*}
where the first factor in the second line can be bounded by $2+2\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[W^2 \mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}]}^3$.
\end{proof}



\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:hub-to-hub-edges2}]
For $A\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R})$ and $s\ge1$ we obtain
\begin{align*}
\mathbf{L}_s(A):=& \frac{1}{2} \mathbb{E} \sum_{(x_1,x_2)\in\eta^2_{\neq}} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{x_1\leftrightarrow x_2, \frac{x_1+x_2}{2}\in \Gamma_s, W_{x_1},W_{x_2}\geq t_s, t_s^{-\frac{2}{d}} \|x_1-x_2\| \in A \bigg\} \\
= & \frac{\gamma^2}{2} \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^2} \mathbb{E}[\varphi(W_1W_2/\|x_1-x_2\|^d) \mathbf{1}\{W_1,W_2\geq t_s\}] \\
& \hspace{2cm} \times \mathbf{1}\bigg\{ \frac{x_1+x_2}{2}\in \Gamma_s, t_s^{-\frac{2}{d}}\|x_1-x_2\|\in A \bigg\} \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2) \\
= & \frac{\gamma^2s}{2} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \mathbb{E}[\varphi(W_1W_2/\|\tilde{v}\|^d) \mathbf{1}\{W_1,W_2\geq t_s\}] \mathbf{1}\{t_s^{-\frac{2}{d}}\|\tilde{v}\|\in A\} \, \mathrm{d}\tilde{v} \\
= & \frac{\gamma^2s}{2} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{W_1,W_2\geq t_s\} \mathbf{1}\{t_s^{-\frac{2}{d}} (W_1W_2)^{1/d}\|v\|\in A\} W_1W_2] \varphi(1/\|v\|^d) \, \mathrm{d}v.
\end{align*}
Since, by the properties of the Pareto distribution $W/t_s$ conditioned on $W\geq t_s$ has the same distribution as $W$, we have
\begin{align*}
& \mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{W_1,W_2\geq t_s\} \mathbf{1}\{t_s^{-\frac{2}{d}} (W_1W_2)^{1/d}\|v\|\in A\} W_1W_2] \\
& = \mathbb{P}(W_1,W_2\geq t_s) t_s^2 \mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{(W_1W_2)^{1/d}\|v\|\in A\} W_1W_2].
\end{align*}
Together with
$$
\mathbb{E}[W\mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}] = \int_{t_s}^\infty u \beta u^{-\beta-1} \, \mathrm{d}u = \frac{\beta}{\beta-1}t_s^{1-\beta} = \frac{\beta}{\beta-1}t_s \mathbb{P}(W\geq t_s)
$$
and the assumption on $(t_s)_{s\geq 1}$ we see that
\begin{align*}
& s \mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{W_1,W_2\geq t_s\} \mathbf{1}\{t_s^{-\frac{2}{d}} (W_1W_2)^{1/d}\|v\|\in A\} W_1W_2] \\
& = \frac{(\beta-1)^2}{\beta^2} s \mathbb{E}[W\mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}]^2 \mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{(W_1W_2)^{1/d}\|v\|\in A\} W_1W_2] \\
& = \frac{(\beta-1)^2a^2}{\beta^2} \mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{(W_1W_2)^{1/d}\|v\|\in A\} W_1W_2].
\end{align*}
This shows that $\mathbf{L}_s(A)=\mathbf{L}(A)$.

As in the proof of Theorem \ref{thm:hub-to-hub-edges} we obtain that
\begin{align*}
& \gamma^3 \mathbb{E} \int_{(\mathbb{R}^d)^3} \mathbf{1}\bigg\{ \frac{x_1+x_2}{2},\frac{x_1+x_3}{2} \in\Gamma_s \bigg\} \mathbf{1}\{W_1,W_2,W_3\geq t_s\} \varphi(W_1W_2/\|x_1-x_2\|^d) \\
& \hspace{2cm} \times \varphi(W_1W_3/\|x_1-x_3\|^d) \, \mathrm{d}(x_1,x_2,x_3) \\
& \leq \gamma^3 c_\varphi^2 s \mathbb{E}[W\mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}]^2 \mathbb{E}[W^2\mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}] = \gamma^3 c_\varphi^2 a^2 \mathbb{E}[W^2\mathbf{1}\{W\geq t_s\}] = \frac{\gamma^3 c_\varphi^2 a^2 \beta}{2-\beta}t_s^{2-\beta}.
\end{align*}
Now the statement follows from Corollary \ref{compound}.
\end{proof}

\section{Related work on the RCM}

\begin{itemize}
\item \cite{Last_Nestmann_Schulte}: component counts, study of functionals of edge-marked Poisson processes

\item \cite{LiuPrivault}: Cumulant bounds for subgraph counts

\item \cite{Heerten_Hirsch_Otto}: Cumulant bounds for subgraph counts

\item \cite{Hirsch_Lachieze-Rey_Takashi_Owada}: Normal approximation for subgraph counts in the age-dependent random connection model

\item \cite{rs2,rs3,scs}: Extreme value limits for maximum edge-lengths

\end{itemize}
\section{To-Do-List}
\begin{itemize}
\item List of notation
\begin{enumerate}
\item vertex set $\eta$ (stationary intensity measure $\gamma\cdot\lambda_d$, $\gamma>0$) 
\item window $\Gamma_s:=s^{1/d}\Gamma$ with $\Gamma$ such that $\lambda_d(\Gamma)=1$, maybe $\Gamma$ compact and convex
\item $\eta_s:=\eta\cap {\Gamma_s}$
\item (unweighed) RCM with connection function $\varphi$ (translation-invariant)
$$\int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varphi(z)\mathrm{d}z<\infty,$$
$\varphi(x,y)=\varphi(x-y)=\varphi(y-x)$ (more general than $\varphi(x,y)=\varphi(\|x-y\|)$)
moment assumption hopefully sufficient for normal approximation of subgraph counts, stronger moment assumptions for edge-length functionals, for example
$$\int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varphi(z) \|z\|^{4\tau} \mathrm{d}z<\infty.$$
$\tau$ for edge-length functionals.
\item Weighted random connection model
$$
\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \int_{[0,\infty)^2} \varphi(z,w_1,w_2) \mathbb{W}^2(d(w_1,w_2)) \, dz <\infty.
$$
\item subgraph counting with non-integrable connection function
\item Parametric weighted RCM $(\eta,\varphi,W)$, $\mathbb{W}$ for the distribution of mark $W$, $W_x$ for the weight of $x\in\eta$, 
$$\IP(W>t)=\ell(t)t^{-\alpha},$$
with $\ell$ being a slowly varying function, special form of $\varphi$,
$$
\varphi(x,y,W_x,W_y)= \phi(\kappa(W_x,W_y)/\|x-y\|)
$$
checking the existing notations in the literature. 
Subgraph counting could require assumptions like
$$
\mathbb{E}[\kappa(W_1,W_2)^{\beta_G}]<\infty
$$
with $\beta_G$ depending on the considered subgraph $G$
\end{enumerate}

\item application for hub-to-hub edges of weighted RCM (power law weights): counts of edges (normal and Poisson approximation) and sums of edges lengths (normal, ?$\alpha$-stable? and compound Poisson approximation)
\item Poisson approximation (modifications needed), compound Poisson approximation and $\alpha$-stable approximation
\item Concentration inequality and moderate deviation (last)
\item introduce Poisson $U$-statistics 
\end{itemize}
